F(t)=[\xi^{t}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}\partial_{t}^{\sigma}x^{t}-\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}\partial_{t}^{\sigma}\psi(t,x,\xi)]_{x=x_{0},\xi=\xi_{0}}.

We handle the case
\sigma\geq 1
first. We compute

\begin{split}&\quad\xi^{t}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}\partial_{t}^{\sigma-1}(\partial_{\xi}a(x^{t},t;\xi^{t}))-\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}\partial_{t}^{\sigma-1}[\xi^{t}\partial_{\xi}a(x^{t},t;\xi^{t})-a]\\
&=-(\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}\partial_{t}^{\sigma-1}\xi^{t})\partial_{\xi}a(x^{t},t;\xi^{t})-\text{mixed der.}+\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}\partial_{t}^{\sigma-1}a.\end{split}

For the mixed terms we have

\text{mixed der.}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\beta_{1},\\
\beta_{2},\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\end{subarray}}(\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{1}}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta_{1}}\partial_{t}^{\sigma_{1}}\xi^{t})(\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{2}}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta_{2}}\partial_{t}^{\sigma_{2}}\partial_{\xi}a)

with the constraints
\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}=\sigma-1
,
\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}=\alpha
,
\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}=\beta
,
|\alpha_{1}|+|\beta_{1}|+|\sigma_{1}|\geq 1
, and
|\alpha_{2}|+|\beta_{2}|+|\sigma_{2}|\geq 1
. Noting that
\partial_{\xi}a=\partial_{t}x^{t}
and applying Lemma
4.10
yields

|\text{mixed der.}|\lesssim R^{\frac{-|\alpha_{1}|+|\beta_{1}|-\sigma_{1}-1}{2}}R^{\frac{-|\alpha_{2}|+|\beta_{2}|-(\sigma_{2}+1)+1}{2}}=R^{\frac{-|\alpha|+|\beta|-\sigma}{2}}.

This makes the contribution of the mixed terms admissible.
For the contribution of the first term we can apply Lemma
4.10
and symbol regularity:

|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}\partial_{t}^{\sigma-1}\xi^{t}\partial_{\xi}a|\lesssim R^{\frac{-|\alpha|+|\beta|-(\sigma-1)-1}{2}}=R^{\frac{-|\alpha|+|\beta|-\sigma}{2}}.

It remains to treat the final term.
The expansion is reminiscent of the proof of Lemma
4.8
. We obtain for
\partial_{t}^{\sigma-1}a
by the chain rule:

\begin{split}&\sum_{\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\kappa_{3}}\big(\partial_{x}^{\kappa_{1}}\partial_{\xi}^{\kappa_{2}}\partial_{t}^{\kappa_{3}}a\big)\big[\prod_{k=1}^{|\kappa_{1}|}\partial_{t}^{\kappa_{1}^{k}}x^{t}\prod_{k=1}^{|\kappa_{2}|}\partial_{t}^{\kappa_{2}^{k}}\xi^{t}\big];\quad 0\leq|\kappa_{1}|,|\kappa_{2}|,|\kappa_{3}|\leq\sigma-1,\\
&\quad\quad\sum_{k=1}^{|\kappa_{1}|}\kappa_{1}^{k}+\sum_{k=1}^{|\kappa_{2}|}\kappa_{2}^{k}+\kappa_{3}=\sigma-1.\end{split}

Next, we apply
\partial_{x}^{\alpha}
, which leads to terms

\begin{split}&\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\\
\alpha_{11},\alpha_{12}\end{subarray}}(\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{11}}\partial_{\xi}^{\alpha_{12}}\partial_{x}^{\kappa_{1}}\partial_{\xi}^{\kappa_{2}}\partial_{t}^{\kappa_{3}}a)\cdot\big[\prod_{k=1}^{|\alpha_{11}|}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{11}^{k}}x^{t}\prod_{k=1}^{|\alpha_{12}|}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{12}^{k}}\xi^{t}\big]\cdot\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{2}}\big[\prod_{k=1}^{|\kappa_{1}|}\partial_{t}^{\kappa_{1}^{k}}x^{t}\prod_{k=1}^{|\kappa_{2}|}\partial_{t}^{\kappa_{2}^{k}}\xi^{t}\big]\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\\
\alpha_{11},\alpha_{12}\end{subarray}}(\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{11}}\partial_{\xi}^{\alpha_{12}}\partial_{x}^{\kappa_{1}}\partial_{\xi}^{\kappa_{2}}\partial_{t}^{\kappa_{3}}a)\cdot\big[\prod_{k=1}^{|\alpha_{11}|}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{11}^{k}}x^{t}\prod_{k=1}^{|\alpha_{12}|}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{12}^{k}}\xi^{t}\big]\\
&\quad\quad\cdot\big[\prod_{k=1}^{|\kappa_{1}|}\partial_{t}^{\kappa_{1}^{k}}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{21}^{k}}x^{t}\prod_{k=1}^{|\kappa_{2}|}\partial_{t}^{\kappa_{2}^{k}}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{22}^{k}}\xi^{t}\big]\end{split}

with

\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}=\alpha,\,\sum_{k=1}^{|\alpha_{11}|}|\alpha_{11}^{k}|+\sum_{k=1}^{|\alpha_{12}|}|\alpha_{12}^{k}|=|\alpha_{1}|,\,\sum_{k=1}^{|\kappa_{1}|}|\alpha_{21}^{k}|+\sum_{k=1}^{|\kappa_{2}|}|\alpha_{22}^{k}|=|\alpha_{2}|.

Finally, we apply
\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}
to the above terms to find

\begin{split}&\partial_{\xi}^{\beta_{1}}(\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{11}}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta_{12}}\partial_{x}^{\kappa_{1}}\partial_{\xi}^{\kappa_{2}}\partial_{t}^{\kappa_{3}}a)\cdot\partial_{\xi}^{\beta_{2}}\big[\prod_{k=1}^{|\alpha_{11}|}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{11}^{k}}x^{t}\prod_{k=1}^{|\alpha_{12}|}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{12}^{k}}\xi^{t}\big]\\
&\quad\quad\cdot\partial_{\xi}^{\beta_{3}}\big[\prod_{k=1}^{|\kappa_{1}|}\partial_{t}^{\kappa_{1}^{k}}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{21}^{k}}x^{t}\prod_{k=1}^{|\kappa_{2}|}\partial_{t}^{\kappa_{2}^{k}}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{22}^{k}}\xi^{t}\big]\\
&=\sum(\partial_{x}^{\beta_{11}}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta_{12}}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{11}}\partial_{\xi}^{\alpha_{12}}\partial_{x}^{\kappa_{1}}\partial_{\xi}^{\kappa_{2}}\partial_{t}^{\kappa_{3}}a)\cdot\big(\prod_{k=1}^{|\beta_{11}|}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta_{11}^{k}}x^{t}\prod_{k=1}^{|\beta_{12}|}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta_{12}^{k}}\xi^{t}\big)\\
&\quad\cdot\big(\prod_{k=1}^{|\alpha_{11}|}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta_{21}^{k}}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{11}^{k}}x^{t}\prod_{k=1}^{|\alpha_{12}|}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta_{22}^{k}}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{12}^{k}}\xi^{t}\big)\cdot\big(\prod_{k=1}^{|\kappa_{1}|}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta_{31}^{k}}\partial_{t}^{\kappa_{1}^{k}}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{21}^{k}}x^{t}\prod_{k=1}^{|\kappa_{2}|}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta_{32}^{k}}\partial_{t}^{\kappa_{2}^{k}}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{22}^{k}}\xi^{t}\big).\end{split}

The conditions on
\beta
read

•

\beta=\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}+\beta_{3}
,

•

\sum_{k=1}^{|\beta_{11}|}|\beta_{11}^{k}|+\sum_{k=1}^{|\beta_{12}|}|\beta_{12}^{k}|=|\beta_{1}|
,

•

\sum_{k=1}^{|\alpha_{11}|}|\beta_{21}^{k}|+\sum_{k=1}^{|\alpha_{12}|}|\beta_{22}^{k}|=|\beta_{2}|
,

•

\sum_{k=1}^{|\kappa_{1}|}|\beta_{31}^{k}|+\sum_{k=1}^{|\kappa_{2}|}|\beta_{32}^{k}|=|\beta_{3}|
.

We obtain the
R
-dependent bounds:

(4.77)

\begin{split}|\cdot|_{\alpha,\beta,\sigma}&\lesssim R^{-|\beta_{11}|-|\alpha_{11}|-|\kappa_{1}|-|\kappa_{3}|}R^{\frac{(|\alpha_{11}|+|\beta_{11}|+|\kappa_{1}|+|\kappa_{3}|-2)_{+}}{2}}\\
&\quad\times R^{\sum_{k=1}^{|\beta_{11}|}\frac{|\beta_{11}^{k}|-1}{2}}R^{\sum_{k=1}^{|\beta_{12}|}\frac{|\beta_{12}^{k}|-1}{2}}\\
&\quad\times R^{\sum_{k=1}^{|\alpha_{11}|}\frac{|\beta_{21}^{k}|-|\alpha_{11}^{k}|+1}{2}}R^{\sum_{k=1}^{|\alpha_{12}|}\frac{|\beta_{22}^{k}|-|\alpha_{12}^{k}|-1}{2}}\\
&\quad\times R^{\sum_{k=1}^{|\kappa_{1}|}\frac{|\beta_{31}^{k}|-|\kappa_{1}^{k}|-|\alpha_{12}^{k}|+1}{2}}R^{\sum_{k=1}^{|\kappa_{2}|}\frac{|\beta_{32}^{k}|-|\kappa_{2}^{k}|-|\alpha_{22}^{k}|-1}{2}}\\
&\lesssim R^{-|\beta_{11}|-|\alpha_{11}|-|\kappa_{1}|-|\kappa_{3}|}R^{\frac{(|\alpha_{11}|+|\beta_{11}|+|\kappa_{1}|+|\kappa_{3}|-2)_{+}}{2}}R^{\frac{|\beta_{1}|}{2}+\frac{|\beta_{11}|}{2}-\frac{|\beta_{12}|}{2}}\\
&\quad\times R^{\frac{|\beta_{2}|}{2}-\frac{|\alpha_{1}|}{2}+\frac{|\alpha_{11}|}{2}-\frac{|\alpha_{12}|}{2}}R^{\frac{|\beta_{3}|}{2}-\frac{|\alpha_{2}|}{2}-\frac{\sum_{k}|\kappa_{1}^{k}|}{2}-\frac{\sum|\kappa_{2}^{k}|}{2}+\frac{|\kappa_{1}|}{2}-\frac{|\kappa_{2}|}{2}}.\end{split}

In case
|\alpha_{11}|+|\beta_{11}|+|\kappa_{1}|+|\kappa_{3}|\geq 2
, the expression simplifies to

|\cdot|_{\alpha,\beta,\sigma}\lesssim R^{\frac{-|\alpha|+|\beta|-\sigma-1}{2}},

which yields an acceptable contribution.

Case A:

|\beta_{11}|=|\alpha_{11}|=|\kappa_{1}|=|\kappa_{3}|=0
.

The above simplifies to

|\cdot|_{\alpha,\beta,\sigma}\lesssim R^{\frac{-|\alpha|+|\beta|-|\sigma|+1}{2}-\frac{|\kappa_{2}|}{2}-\frac{|\alpha_{12}|}{2}-\frac{|\beta_{12}|}{2}}.

Case A.I:
For
|\kappa_{2}|+|\alpha_{12}|+|\beta_{12}|\geq 1
, the estimate suffices.

Case A.II:

|\kappa_{2}|=0
,
|\alpha_{12}|=|\beta_{12}|=0
. In this case we find
\sigma=1
and
|\alpha|=|\beta|=0
, which is not considered here as this amounts to only time derivatives, which was handled above.

Finally, let us turn to
\sigma=0
and
(|\alpha|,|\beta|)\neq(1,0)
as the case
(|\alpha|,|\beta|)=(1,0)
was handled above.
We consider again

F(t)=[\xi^{t}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}x^{t}-\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}\psi(t,x,\xi)]_{x=x_{0},\xi=\xi_{0}}.

It seems difficult to estimate this directly. So, we observe that
F(0)=0
and estimate the time-derivative by
|F^{\prime}(t)|\lesssim R^{\frac{-|\alpha|+|\beta|-2}{2}}
.
We compute (and omit the evaluation to ease notation)

(4.78)

\begin{split}F^{\prime}(t)&=-\partial_{x}a\cdot\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}x^{t}+\xi^{t}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}\partial_{t}x^{t}-\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}[\xi^{t}\partial_{\xi}a]+\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}a\\
&=-\partial_{x}a\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}x^{t}-\sum_{(*)}(\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{1}}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta_{1}}\xi^{t})(\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{2}}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta_{2}}(\partial_{\xi}a))+\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}a.\end{split}

The summation restricted to
(*)
refers to
\alpha_{1},\beta_{1},\alpha_{2},\beta_{2}
with
|\alpha_{1}|+|\beta_{1}|\neq 0
, noting that the complementary term cancels the second term of the first line.

In the following we expand
\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}a
and see that the terms, which do not cancel with the first and second term, contribute only to order
R^{\frac{-|\alpha|+|\beta|-2}{2}}
.

We treat
\beta=0
and
|\beta|\geq 1
separately. We first consider
\beta=0
. In this case we expand

\begin{split}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}a&=\partial_{x}^{\alpha}a=\sum_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{1}}\partial_{\xi}^{\alpha_{2}}\prod_{k=1}^{|\alpha_{1}|}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{1}^{k}}x^{t}\prod_{k=1}^{|\alpha_{2}|}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{2}^{k}}\xi^{t}\\
&=\partial_{x}a\partial_{x}^{\alpha}x^{t}+\partial_{\xi}a\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\xi^{t}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\\
|\alpha_{1}|+|\alpha_{2}|\geq 2\end{subarray}}(\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{1}}\partial_{\xi}^{\alpha_{2}}a)\prod_{k=1}^{|\alpha_{1}|}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{1}^{k}}x^{t}\prod_{k=1}^{|\alpha_{2}|}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{2}^{k}}\xi^{t}.\end{split}

The first and second expression cancels with the first and second term in (
4.78
). For the third expression we estimate by the symbol regularity and Lemma
4.10
:

\lesssim R^{-|\alpha_{1}|}R^{\frac{(|\alpha_{1}|-2)_{+}}{2}}R^{\sum_{k=1}^{|\alpha_{1}|}\frac{-|\alpha_{1}^{k}|}{2}+\frac{1}{2}}R^{\sum_{k=1}^{|\alpha_{2}|}\frac{-|\alpha_{2}^{k}|}{2}-\frac{1}{2}}.

For
|\alpha_{1}|\geq 2
this is acceptable by symbol regularity. For
\alpha_{1}=0
this is
\lesssim R^{-\frac{|\alpha|}{2}-\frac{|\alpha_{2}|}{2}}
, which is acceptable because
|\alpha_{2}|\geq 2
. For
|\alpha_{1}|=1
we find
\lesssim R^{-1}R^{-\frac{|\alpha|}{2}}R^{\frac{1}{2}}R^{-\frac{|\alpha_{2}|}{2}}
, again acceptable by
|\alpha_{2}|\geq 2
.

We turn to the case
|\beta|\geq 1
, for which we expand

\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}a=\sum_{\beta_{1},\beta_{2}}\partial_{x}^{\beta_{1}}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta_{2}}a\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{|\beta_{1}|}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta_{1}^{k}}x^{t}\prod_{k=1}^{|\beta_{2}|}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta_{2}}\xi^{t}\text{ with }\sum_{k}|\beta_{1}^{k}|+\sum_{j}|\beta_{2}^{j}|=|\beta|.

This is split as

(4.79)

(\partial_{\xi}a)\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}\xi^{t}+(\partial_{x}a)\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}x^{t}+\sum_{|\beta_{1}|+|\beta_{2}|\geq 2}(\partial_{x}^{\beta_{1}}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta_{2}}a)\big(\prod_{k=1}^{|\beta_{1}|}x^{t}\big)\big(\prod_{k=1}^{|\beta_{2}|}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta_{2}}\xi^{t}\big).

It remains to apply
\partial_{x}^{\alpha}
: For the first two terms we find

(4.80)

\sum_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{1}}(\partial_{\xi}a)\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{2}}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}\xi^{t}+(\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{1}}(\partial_{x}a))\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{2}}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}x^{t}.

The terms with
|\alpha_{1}|=0
in (
4.80
) are cancelled by the first term in (
4.78
) and the second term in (
4.78
) with the condition on the summation
|\alpha_{2}|+|\beta_{2}|=0
.

First expression in (
4.80
):
The single terms are given by

\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{1}}(\partial_{\xi}a)(\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{2}}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}\xi^{t})=\sum_{\alpha_{11},\alpha_{12}}(\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{11}}\partial_{\xi}^{\alpha_{12}}\partial_{\xi}a)\big(\prod_{k=1}^{|\alpha_{11}}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{11}^{k}}x^{t}\big)\big(\prod_{k=1}^{|\alpha_{12}|}(\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{12}^{k}}\xi^{t})\big(\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{2}}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}\xi^{t}\big).

We have the conditions
|\alpha_{11}|+|\alpha_{12}|\geq 1
,
\sum_{k=1}^{|\alpha_{11}|}|\alpha_{11}^{k}|+\sum_{k=1}^{|\alpha_{12}^{k}|}|\alpha_{12}^{k}|=|\alpha_{1}|
. We estimate a single term
|\cdot|_{\alpha,\beta}
by symbol regularity and Lemma
4.10
:

\begin{split}|\cdot|_{\alpha,\beta}|&\lesssim R^{-|\alpha_{11}|}R^{\frac{(|\alpha_{11}|-2)_{+}}{2}}R^{\sum_{k=1}^{|\alpha_{11}|}(-\frac{|\alpha_{11}^{k}|}{2}+\frac{1}{2})}R^{\sum_{k=1}^{|\alpha_{12}|}(-\frac{|\alpha_{12}^{k}|}{2}-\frac{1}{2})}R^{\frac{-|\alpha_{2}|+|\beta|-1}{2}}\\
&\lesssim R^{-|\alpha_{11}|}R^{\frac{(|\alpha_{11}|-2)_{+}}{2}}R^{-\frac{|\alpha_{1}|}{2}+\frac{|\alpha_{11}|}{2}-\frac{|\alpha_{12}|}{2}}R^{\frac{-|\alpha_{2}|+|\beta|-1}{2}}.\end{split}

Now we can conclude with a case-by-case analysis. For
|\alpha_{11}|\geq 2
the expression is found to be
|\cdot|_{\alpha,\beta}\lesssim R^{\frac{-|\alpha|+|\beta|-2}{2}}
. For
|\alpha_{11}|=0
we find for a single term

|\cdot|_{\alpha,\beta}\lesssim R^{\sum_{k=1}^{|\alpha_{12}|}(-\frac{|\alpha_{12}^{k}|}{2}-\frac{1}{2})}R^{\frac{-|\alpha_{2}|+|\beta|-1}{2}}\lesssim R^{\frac{-|\alpha|+|\beta|-2}{2}}.

In the ultimate estimate we used
|\alpha_{1}|\geq 1
by assumption such that
|\alpha_{12}|\geq 1
.
Similarly, the term with
|\alpha_{11}|=1
can be estimated:

\begin{split}|\cdot|_{\alpha,\beta}&\lesssim R^{-1}R^{-\frac{|\alpha_{1}|}{2}}R^{\frac{1}{2}}R^{-\frac{|\alpha_{12}|}{2}}R^{\frac{-|\alpha_{2}|+|\beta|-1}{2}}\\
&\lesssim R^{\frac{-|\alpha|+|\beta|-2}{2}}.\end{split}

Second expression in (
4.80
)
: we have, recalling that
|\alpha_{1}|\geq 1
:

\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{1}}(\partial_{x}a)\partial_{x_{2}}^{\alpha_{2}}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}x^{t}=\sum_{\alpha_{11},\alpha_{12}}(\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{11}}\partial_{\xi}^{\alpha_{12}}\partial_{x}a)\big(\prod_{k=1}^{|\alpha_{11}|}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{11}^{k}}x^{t}\big)\big(\prod_{k=1}^{|\alpha_{12}|}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{12}^{k}}\xi^{t}\big)\big(\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{2}}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}x^{t}\big).

The indices satisfy
\sum_{k=1}^{|\alpha_{11}|}|\alpha_{11}^{k}|+\sum_{k=1}^{|\alpha_{12}|}|\alpha_{12}^{k}|=|\alpha_{1}|\geq 1
.
We obtain for a single term the estimate:

\begin{split}|\cdot|_{\alpha,\beta}&\lesssim R^{-|\alpha_{11}|-1}R^{\frac{(|\alpha_{11}|-1)_{+}}{2}}R^{\frac{-|\alpha_{1}|+|\alpha_{11}|-|\alpha_{12}|}{2}}R^{-\frac{|\alpha_{2}|}{2}+\frac{|\beta|}{2}-\frac{1}{2}}\\
&\lesssim R^{-|\alpha_{11}|-1}R^{\frac{(|\alpha_{11}|-1)_{+}}{2}}R^{-\frac{|\alpha|}{2}+\frac{|\alpha_{11}|}{2}-\frac{|\alpha_{12}|}{2}+\frac{|\beta|}{2}-\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

It is straightforward that
|\alpha_{11}|\geq 1
gives an acceptable contribution, and we check that
\alpha_{11}=0
likewise.

We have taken care of the first and second term in (
4.79
). It remains to estimate the third term, for which holds
|\beta_{1}|+|\beta_{2}|\geq 2
:

\begin{split}&\quad\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\big[(\partial_{x}^{\beta_{1}}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta_{2}}a)\big(\prod_{k=1}^{|\beta_{1}|}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta_{1}^{k}}x^{t}\big)\big(\prod_{k=1}^{|\beta_{2}|}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta_{2}^{k}}\xi^{t}\big)\big]\\
&=\sum_{\alpha_{11},\alpha_{12},\ldots}(\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{11}}\partial_{\xi}^{\alpha_{12}}\partial_{x}^{\beta_{1}}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta_{2}}a)\prod_{k=1}^{|\alpha_{11}|}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{11}^{k}}x^{t}\prod_{k=1}^{|\alpha_{12}|}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{12}^{k}}\xi^{t}\prod_{k=1}^{|\beta_{1}|}(\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{21}^{k}}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta_{1}^{k}}x^{t}\big)\prod_{k=1}^{|\beta_{2}|}\big(\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{22}^{k}}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta_{2}^{k}}\xi^{t}\big)\end{split}

and additionally,

|\alpha_{1}|+|\alpha_{2}|=|\alpha|,\,\sum_{k}|\alpha_{11}^{k}|+\sum_{k}|\alpha_{12}^{k}|=|\alpha_{1}|,\,\sum_{k=1}^{|\beta_{1}|}|\alpha_{21}^{k}|+\sum_{k=1}^{|\beta_{2}|}|\alpha_{22}^{k}|=|\alpha_{2}|.

This allows us to estimate a single term as

|\cdot|_{\alpha,\beta}\lesssim R^{-|\alpha_{11}|-|\beta_{1}|}R^{\frac{(|\alpha_{11}|+|\beta_{1}|-2)_{+}}{2}}R^{-\frac{|\alpha_{1}|}{2}}R^{\frac{|\alpha_{11}|-|\alpha_{12}|}{2}}R^{-\frac{|\alpha_{2}|}{2}}R^{\frac{|\beta|}{2}}R^{\frac{|\beta_{1}|}{2}}R^{-\frac{|\beta_{2}|}{2}}.

For
|\beta_{1}|\geq 2
the expression is acceptable by the symbol regularity, and more generally for
|\beta_{11}|+|\alpha_{11}|\geq 2
. In case
|\beta_{1}|=1
and
|\alpha_{11}|=0
the estimate

|\cdot|_{\alpha,\beta}\lesssim R^{-1}R^{-\frac{|\alpha_{1}|}{2}}R^{-\frac{|\alpha_{12}|}{2}}R^{-\frac{|\alpha_{2}|}{2}}R^{\frac{|\beta|}{2}}R^{\frac{|\beta_{1}|}{2}}R^{-\frac{|\beta_{2}|}{2}}\lesssim R^{\frac{-|\alpha|+|\beta|}{2}}R^{-1}.

Finally, we estimate for
|\beta_{1}|=0
,
|\beta_{2}|\geq 2

|\cdot|_{\alpha,\beta}\lesssim R^{-|\alpha_{11}|}R^{\frac{(|\alpha_{11}|-2)_{+}}{2}}R^{-\frac{|\alpha_{1}|}{2}}R^{\frac{|\alpha_{11}|-|\alpha_{12}|}{2}}R^{-\frac{|\alpha_{2}|}{2}}R^{\frac{|\beta|}{2}}R^{-1}.

From Case-By-Case analysis for
|\alpha_{11}|=0
,
|\alpha_{11}|=1
,
|\alpha_{11}|\geq 2
, we see that the expression is always estimated
\lesssim R^{\frac{-|\alpha|}{2}+\frac{|\beta|}{2}-1}
.

We have proved that
|F^{\prime}(t)|\lesssim R^{\frac{-|\alpha|+|\beta|+|\sigma|-2}{2}}
,
which completes the proof by integrating over
|t|\lesssim R
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
4.3
.

With the above lemma at hand, it suffices to show regularity and decay for the modified function:

G_{1}(t,x,\xi,y)=C_{R}^{2}e^{-i\xi_{0}^{t}(y-x_{0}^{t})}e^{-i\psi(t,x_{0},\xi_{0})}(S(t,0)(e^{i\xi_{0}(x-x_{0})}\phi_{x,\xi}))(y).

We translate
G_{1}
to the origin by

G_{2}(t,x,\xi,y)=G_{1}(t,x_{0}+x,\xi_{0}+\xi,x_{0}^{t}+y).

We find that
G_{2}
solves

(D_{t}+a_{2}^{w}(t,y,D_{y}))G_{2}=0,\quad G_{2}(0)=C_{R}^{2}\phi_{x,\xi}=C_{R}^{2}e^{i\xi(x-y)}e^{-r(y-x)^{2}},

where

a_{2}(t,y,\eta)=a(t,x_{0}^{t}+y,\xi_{0}^{t}+\eta)-a(t,x_{0}^{t},\xi_{0}^{t})-ya_{x}(t,x_{0}^{t},\xi_{0}^{t})-\eta a_{\xi}(t,x_{0}^{t},\xi_{0}^{t}).

For this function it suffices to prove for that for times
|t|\lesssim R
we have

(4.81)

\||(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)^{\gamma}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{\sigma}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}\partial_{y}^{\nu}G_{2}(t,x,\xi,y)|\big|_{x=0,\xi=0}\|_{2}\leq c_{\gamma,\alpha,\beta,\nu,\sigma}R^{\frac{-|\alpha|+|\beta|-|\nu|-|\sigma|}{2}}C_{R}^{2}\|\phi_{x,\xi}\|_{2}.

The notation
c_{\gamma,\alpha,\beta,\nu,\sigma}
indicates that for any
N
such that
|\gamma|+|\alpha|+|\beta|+|\nu|+|\sigma|\leq N
we can find a constant
c
such that (
4.81
) holds uniformly in
R\geq 1
.
The proof of Proposition
4.3
will be complete, once we have established (
4.81
).
∎

Proof of (
4.81
).

We note that
\phi_{x,\xi}
is a Schwartz function with derivatives in
x
and
\xi
at the origin satisfying the bounds

\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}\phi_{x,\xi}|_{x=0,\xi=0}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim R^{(-|\alpha|+|\beta|)/2}\|\phi_{0,0}\|_{2}.

Here it is important to have centered phase space variables. This handles the derivatives in
x
and
\xi
. It remains to check derivatives in
y
,
t
, and spatial confinement.

To show (
4.81
), we use energy estimates for
L^{2}
-normalized Schwartz functions

(D_{t}-a_{2}^{w}(y,D))v=0,\quad v(0)=v_{0},

and prove

\|(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{t})^{\gamma}(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)^{\alpha}(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y})^{\beta}v(t)\|_{2}\lesssim\sum_{|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq|\alpha|+|\beta|+2|\gamma|}\|(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)^{\alpha^{\prime}}(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y})^{\beta^{\prime}}v(0)\|_{2}.

Recall that
|T|\leq R
.
In the first step we show

(4.82)

\|(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)^{\alpha}(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y})^{\beta}v(t)\|_{2}\lesssim\sum_{|\bar{\alpha}|+|\bar{\beta}|\leq|\alpha|+|\beta|}\|(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)^{\bar{\alpha}}(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y})^{\bar{\beta}}v_{0}\|_{2}.

Time regularity is considered further below. We prove the estimate by induction on
|\alpha|+|\beta|
. For
|\alpha|+|\beta|=1
we shall prove that for

A(t)=\|R^{-\frac{1}{2}}yv(t)\|_{2}^{2}+\|R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\eta}v\|_{2}^{2}+\|v(t)\|_{2}^{2})

we have

\frac{d}{dt}A(t)\lesssim R^{-1}A(t)

and the claim follows from Grønwall’s inequality.
The above will follow from bounds

(4.83)

\displaystyle\|(D_{t}-a_{2}^{w})(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}yv)\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim R^{-1}(\|v(t)\|_{2}+R^{-\frac{1}{2}}yv(t)\|_{2}+\|R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\eta}v(t)\|_{2})

(4.84)

\displaystyle\|(D_{t}-a_{2}^{w})(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\eta}v)(t)\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim R^{-1}(\|v(t)\|_{2}+\|R^{-\frac{1}{2}}yv(t)\|_{2}+\|R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\eta}v(t)\|_{2}).

Indeed, the first bound implies

\begin{split}\frac{d}{dt}\|R^{-\frac{1}{2}}yv(t)\|_{2}^{2}&=2\Re(\langle R^{-\frac{1}{2}}yv(t),\big(\frac{d}{dt}+ia_{2}^{w}\big)(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}yv(t))\rangle)\\
&\lesssim\|R^{-\frac{1}{2}}yv(t)\|_{2}(R^{-1}(\|v(t)\|_{2}+\|R^{-\frac{1}{2}}yv(t)\|_{2}+\|R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\eta}v(t)\|_{2}))\\
&\lesssim R^{-1}A(t).\end{split}

The first equation follows from self-adjointness of
a_{2}^{w}
, the second one from Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and (
4.83
). Similarly, the second bound implies an estimate
\partial_{t}\|R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\eta}v(t)\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim R^{-1}A(t)
.

To verify (
4.83
) and (
4.84
), we compute the equations satisfied by
R^{-\frac{1}{2}}yv
and
R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y}v
. By taking commutators for the Weyl quantization, like in
[
22
, p. 238]
we have

(4.85)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}(D_{t}-a_{2}^{w}(y,D_{y}))(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}yv)&=-i(\partial_{\eta}a_{2})^{w}(y,D_{y})R^{-\frac{1}{2}}v,\\
(D_{t}-a_{2}^{w}(y,D_{y}))(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y}v)&=iR^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial_{y}a_{2})^{w}(y,D_{y})v.\end{array}\right.

We compute by the fundamental theorem of calculus

(4.86)

\begin{split}(\partial_{\eta}a_{2})(y,\eta)&=a_{\eta}(t,x_{0}^{t}+y,\xi_{0}^{t}+\eta)-a_{\eta}(t,x_{0}^{t},\xi_{0}^{t})\\
&=\int_{0}^{1}a_{\eta y}(t,x_{0}^{t}+sy,\xi_{0}^{t}+s\eta)y+a_{\eta\eta}(t,x_{0}^{t}+sy,\xi_{0}^{t}+s\eta)\cdot\eta ds\end{split}

and note that we can equivalently prove bounds for the standard quantization
\|\partial_{\eta}a_{2}(y,D)R^{-\frac{1}{2}}v\|_{2}+\|R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y}a_{2}(y,D)v\|_{2}
because the difference to Weyl quantization is given by

R^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|(\langle D_{y},D_{\eta}\rangle\partial_{\eta}a_{2})v\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim R^{-1}(\|v(t)\|_{2}+\|R^{-\frac{1}{2}}yv(t)\|_{2}+\|R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\eta}v(t)\|_{2}),

and similarly for the second term.

We plug in the expansion in standard quantization and obtain

(4.87)

\begin{split}(\partial_{\eta}a_{2})(y,D_{y})R^{-\frac{1}{2}}v&=C_{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{-iy\eta}\int_{0}^{1}a_{\eta y}(t,x_{0}^{t}+sy,\xi_{0}^{t}+s\eta)yR^{-\frac{1}{2}}\hat{v}(\eta)dsd\eta\\
&\quad+C_{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{-iy\eta}\int_{0}^{1}a_{\eta y}(t,x_{0}^{t}+sy,\xi_{0}^{t}+s\eta)\eta R^{-\frac{1}{2}}\hat{v}(\eta)dsd\eta.\end{split}

The first term in (
4.87
) we write as

\begin{split}&\quad\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{-iy\eta}a_{\eta y}(t,x_{0}^{t}+sy,\xi_{0}^{t}+s\eta)(yR^{-\frac{1}{2}}\hat{v}(\eta))d\eta ds\\
&=\int_{0}^{1}\big(a_{\eta y}(t,x_{0}^{t}+sy,\xi_{0}^{t}+sD_{y})R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y\big)(y,D_{y})vds.\end{split}

We use symbol composition to write

\begin{split}&\quad\|(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}ya_{\eta y}(t,x_{0}^{t}+sy,\xi_{0}^{t}+sD_{y}))v\|_{2}\\
&\leq\|a_{\eta y}(t,x_{0}^{t}+sy,\xi_{0}^{t}+sD_{y})\circ(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)v\|_{2}+R^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{O}(\|a_{\eta yy}(t,x_{0}^{t}+sy,\xi_{0}^{t}+sD_{y})v\|_{2})\\
&\lesssim R^{-1}(\|R^{-\frac{1}{2}}yv\|_{2}+\|v(t)\|_{2}).\end{split}

The second term in (
4.87
) we write as

\begin{split}&\quad C_{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{-iy\eta}\int_{0}^{1}a_{\eta\eta}(t,x_{0}^{t}+sy,\xi_{0}^{t}+s\eta)(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}\eta\hat{v}(\eta))dsd\eta\\
&=iC_{d}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{-iy\eta}a_{\eta\eta}(t,x_{0}^{t}+sy,\xi_{0}^{t}+s\eta)\big[\widehat{\partial_{y}v}R^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big]d\eta dy\\
&=R^{-1}iC_{d}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{-iy\eta}a_{\eta\eta}(t,x_{0}^{t}+sy,\xi_{0}^{t}+s\eta)\widehat{R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y}v}(\eta)d\eta ds.\end{split}

Since
\|a_{\eta\eta}(t,x_{0}^{t}+sy,\xi_{0}^{t}+sD_{y})\|_{L^{2}\to L^{2}}\lesssim 1
, we obtain

\|C_{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{-iy\eta}\int_{0}^{1}a_{\eta\eta}(t,x_{0}^{t}+sy,\xi_{0}^{t}+s\eta)(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}\eta\hat{v}(\eta))dsd\eta\|_{2}\lesssim R^{-1}\|R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y}v\|_{2}.

This completes the proof of (
4.83
).

We turn to the proof of (
4.84
). By the fundamental theorem we have

\begin{split}\partial_{y}a_{2}(y,\eta)&=a_{y}(t,x_{0}^{t}+y,\xi_{0}^{t}+\eta)-a_{y}(t,x_{0}^{t},\xi_{0}^{t})\\
&=\int_{0}^{1}a_{yy}(t,x_{0}^{t}+sy,\xi_{0}^{t}+s\eta)y+a_{y\eta}(t,x_{0}^{t}+sy,\xi_{0}^{t}+s\eta)\eta ds.\end{split}

Next, we follow along the above argument to show (
4.84
) with the obvious changes. We note that it is admissible to change to standard quantization. Next, we write

(4.88)

\begin{split}R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y}a_{2}(y,D_{y})v&=C_{d}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{iy\cdot\eta}a_{yy}(x_{0}^{t}+sy,t,\xi_{0}^{t}+s\eta)(R^{\frac{1}{2}}y)\hat{v}(\eta)d\eta ds\\
&\quad+C_{d}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{iy\cdot\eta}a_{y\eta}(x_{0}^{t}+sy,t,\xi_{0}^{t}+s\eta)(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\eta)\hat{v}(\eta)d\eta ds.\end{split}

By symbol composition, as above we find

\begin{split}\|R^{\frac{1}{2}}ya_{yy}(x_{0}^{t}+sy,t,\xi_{0}^{t}+sD_{y})v\|_{2}&\lesssim\|a_{yy}(t,x_{0}^{t}+sy,\xi_{0}^{t}+sD_{y})(R^{\frac{1}{2}}yv)\|_{2}\\
&\quad+R^{\frac{1}{2}}\|a_{y\eta\eta}(t,x_{0}^{t}+sy,\xi_{0}^{t}+sD_{y})v\|_{2}\\
&\lesssim R^{-1}(\|R^{-\frac{1}{2}}yv\|_{2}+\|v\|_{2}).\end{split}

This gives an acceptable estimate for the first term in (
4.88
).

For the second term in (
4.88
) we obtain

\begin{split}&\quad C_{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{iy\eta}\int_{0}^{1}a_{\eta y}(t,x_{0}^{t}+sy,\xi_{0}^{t}+s\eta)(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\eta\hat{v}(\eta))dsd\eta\\
&=iC_{d}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{iy\eta}a_{\eta y}(t,x_{0}^{t}+sy,\xi_{0}^{t}+s\eta)\widehat{R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y}v}(\eta)d\eta ds.\end{split}

The
L^{2}
-norm of
a_{\eta y}(t,x_{0}^{t}+sy,\xi_{0}^{t}+sD_{y})
is bounded by
R^{-1}
, which gives the estimate of the above by
R^{-1}\|R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y}v\|_{L^{2}}
. The proof of (
4.84
) is complete.

This proves (
4.82
) for
|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq 1
. We turn to the induction step: Here we expand
(D_{t}+a_{2}^{w})(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)^{\alpha}(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y})^{\beta}v
as commutators. We commute
(D_{t}+a_{2}^{w})
to act on
v
taking advantage of
(D_{t}+a_{2}^{w})v=0
.

In the first step by the commutator of
a_{2}^{w}
and
y
we find

\begin{split}&\quad\|(D_{t}-a_{2}^{w})[(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)^{\alpha-1}(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y})^{\beta}v]\|_{2}\\
&\lesssim\|(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)(D_{t}+a_{2}^{w})(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)^{\alpha-1}(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y})^{\beta}v\|_{2}\\
&\quad+\|R^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\partial_{\eta}a_{2})^{w}(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)^{\alpha-1}(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y})^{\beta}v\|_{2}.\end{split}

Here we abuse notation and denote multiindices
\alpha^{\prime}
with
|\alpha^{\prime}|=|\alpha|-1
by
\alpha-1
.

The second term is readily estimated by the same expansion like above:

\begin{split}&\quad\|R^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\partial_{\eta}a_{2})^{w}(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)^{\alpha^{\prime}}(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial)^{\beta}v\|_{2}\\
&\lesssim R^{-1}(\|(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)^{\alpha-1}(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y})^{\beta}v\|_{2}+\|(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y})(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)^{\alpha-1}(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y})^{\beta}v\|_{2}\\
&\quad+\|(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)^{\alpha-1}(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y})^{\beta}v\|_{2})\text{ and }|\alpha|=|\alpha^{\prime}|+|\alpha^{\prime\prime}|.\end{split}

Next, we shall prove that for
\alpha^{\prime}
,
\alpha^{\prime\prime}
with
|\alpha^{\prime}|\cdot|\alpha^{\prime\prime}|>0
we find

(4.89)

\begin{split}&\quad\|(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)^{\alpha^{\prime}}(D_{t}+a_{2}^{w})(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)^{\alpha^{\prime\prime}}(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y})^{\beta}v\|_{2}\\
&\lesssim\|(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)^{\alpha^{\prime}+1}(D_{t}+a_{2}^{w})(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)^{\alpha^{\prime\prime}-1}(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y})^{\beta}v\|_{2}\\
&\quad+R^{-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bar{\alpha},\bar{\beta},\\
|\bar{\alpha}|+|\bar{\beta}|\leq|\alpha|+|\beta|\end{subarray}}\|(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)^{\bar{\alpha}}(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y})^{\bar{\beta}}v\|_{2}.\end{split}

To this end we induct on
|\alpha^{\prime\prime}|
and plug in the commutator:

[a_{2}^{w},R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y]=R^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\partial_{\eta}a_{2})^{w},

which reduces us to estimate

\|(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)^{\alpha^{\prime}}R^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\partial_{\eta}a_{2})^{w}(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)^{\alpha^{\prime\prime}-1}(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y})^{\beta}v\|_{2}.

We would now like to use the above argument, but before that we commute

R^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\partial_{\eta}a_{2}^{w})
to the end: By another commutator estimate

[R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y,R^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\eta}a_{2}^{w}]=R^{-1}(\partial_{\eta\eta}a_{2})^{w},

which is
R\cdot L^{2}
-bounded. Further commutators with
R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y
are better behaved. We can estimate

\begin{split}&\quad\|(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)^{\alpha^{\prime}}(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\eta}a_{2})^{w}(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)^{\alpha^{\prime\prime}_{-1}}(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y})^{\beta}v\|_{2}\\
&\lesssim\|R^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\partial_{\eta}a_{2})^{w}(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)^{\alpha_{-1}}(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y})^{\beta}v\|_{2}+R^{-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bar{\alpha},\bar{\beta},\\
|\bar{\alpha}|+|\bar{\beta}|\leq|\alpha|+|\beta|\end{subarray}}\|(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)^{\bar{\alpha}}(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y})^{\bar{\beta}}v\|_{2}.\end{split}

The first term can be estimated by the second line by means of the expansion (
4.86
).

By the above, we can reduce the left hand side (
4.89
) to

\|(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)^{\alpha}(D_{t}+a_{2}^{w})(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y})^{\beta}v\|_{2}+\text{l.o.t.}

The strategy is to commute
(D_{t}-a_{2}^{w})
to act on
v
, which is annihilated. We need to compute the commutator with
R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y}
:

[a_{2}^{w},R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y}]=R^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial_{y}a_{2})^{w}.

We want to use the estimate from above, but this requires to commute
R^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial_{y}a_{2})^{w}
after
(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)^{\alpha}
.
We have the commutators

[(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y),R^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial_{y}a_{2})^{w}]=(\partial_{y\eta}a_{2})^{w},

which is
R\cdot L^{2}
-bounded, and moreover

[R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y},R^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial_{y}a_{2})^{w}]=R(\partial_{yy}a_{2})^{w},

which is
R\cdot L^{2}
-bounded as well, and so are further commutators with
R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y
and
R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y}
. We obtain for
|\beta|=|\beta_{1}|+|\beta_{2}|

\begin{split}&\quad\|(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)^{\alpha}(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y})^{\beta_{1}}(D_{t}+a_{2}^{w})(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y})^{\beta_{2}}v\|_{2}\\
&\lesssim R^{-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bar{\alpha},\bar{\beta},\\
|\bar{\alpha}|+|\bar{\beta}|\leq|\alpha|+|\beta|\end{subarray}}\|(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)^{\bar{\alpha}}(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y})^{\bar{\beta}}v(t)\|_{2}.\end{split}

This suffices for (
4.82
) by Grønwall’s inequality.

Finally, we turn to the time regularity. We will successively use the equation to find the estimate

(4.90)

\|(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)^{\alpha}(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y})^{\beta}(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{t})^{\gamma}v(t)\|_{2}\lesssim\sum_{|\alpha^{\prime}|+|\beta^{\prime}|\leq|\alpha|+|\beta|+2|\gamma|}\|(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)^{\alpha^{\prime}}(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y})^{\beta^{\prime}}v(t)\|_{2}.

The right hand side can be handled by the energy arguments from above.
We begin with the case
\gamma=1
,
\alpha=\beta=0
: Since
v
solves
(D_{t}+a_{2}^{w})v=0
, we find
\|R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{t}v\|_{2}=R^{\frac{1}{2}}\|a_{2}^{w}v\|_{2}
. Like above we can change to standard quantization and by Taylor expansion we have

a_{2}(t,y,\eta)=\mathcal{O}(y^{2}\partial_{y}^{2}a+y\eta\partial^{2}_{y\eta}a+\eta^{2}\partial_{\eta}^{2}a).

Now the estimate follows from symbol composition

\|a_{2}(y,D_{y})v\|_{2}\lesssim R^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq 2}\|(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)^{\alpha}(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y})^{\beta}v(t)\|_{2}.

To evaluate higher orders, we plug in the equation to find

(4.91)

\|(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)^{\alpha}(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y})^{\beta}(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{t})^{\gamma}v\|_{2}=R^{\frac{1}{2}}\|((R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)^{\alpha}(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y})^{\beta}(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{t})^{\gamma-1}a_{2}^{w}v\|_{2}.

Now we have to commute
a_{2}^{w}
to the end and need to consider commutators

[R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{t},a_{2}^{w}]=R^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial_{t}a_{2})^{w}
.
\partial_{t}a_{2}
is composed of three terms, which share the same structure:

(4.92)

\begin{split}&\quad\partial_{t}a_{2}(t,y,\eta)\\
&=\big((\partial_{t}a)(t,x_{0}^{t}+y,\xi_{0}^{t}+\eta)-(\partial_{t}a)(t,x_{0}^{t},\xi_{0}^{t})-y\partial^{2}_{tx}a(t,x_{0}^{t},\xi_{0}^{t})-\eta\partial^{2}_{t\eta}a(t,x_{0}^{t},\xi_{0}^{t})\big)\\
&\quad+\big(\partial_{x}a(t,x_{0}^{t}+y,\xi_{0}^{t}+\eta)-\partial_{x}a(t,x_{0}^{t},\xi_{0}^{t})\\
&\quad\quad-y\partial^{2}_{xx}a(t,x_{0}^{t},\xi_{0}^{t})-\eta\partial^{2}_{x\eta}a(t,x_{0}^{t},\xi_{0}^{t})\big)a_{\eta}(t,x_{0}^{t},\xi_{0}^{t})\\
&\quad+\big(\partial_{\eta}a(t,x_{0}^{t}+y,\xi_{0}^{t}+\eta)-\partial_{\eta}a(t,x_{0}^{t},\xi_{0}^{t})\\
&\quad\quad-y\partial^{2}_{x\eta}a(t,x_{0}^{t},\xi_{0}^{t})-\eta\partial^{2}_{\eta\eta}a(t,x_{0}^{t},\xi_{0}^{t})\big)(-a_{x}(t,x_{0}^{t},\xi_{0}^{t})).\end{split}

Note that for all three terms the symbols vanish of second order in
(y,\eta)
; here it suffices to use the first order Taylor expansion.
By the fundamental theorem we have the identity for the symbols:

\begin{split}(A)&=\partial_{t}a(t,x_{0}^{t}+y,\xi_{0}^{t}+\eta)-\partial_{t}a(t,x_{0}^{t},\xi_{0}^{t})\\
&=\int_{0}^{1}\partial^{2}_{tx}a(t,x_{0}^{t}+sy,\xi_{0}^{t}+s\eta)\cdot y+\partial^{2}_{t\eta}a(t,x_{0}^{t}+sy,\xi_{0}^{t}+s\eta)\cdot\eta ds.\end{split}

By changing like above to standard quantization and symbol composition, this gives an estimate:

\|(A)^{w}R^{\frac{1}{2}}v\|_{2}\lesssim R^{-1}(\|R^{-\frac{1}{2}}yv\|_{L^{2}}+\|(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y})v\|_{L^{2}}+\|v\|_{L^{2}}).

The third and fourth term in the first line of the expansion in (
4.92
) can be estimated directly by the regularity of
a
:

\|\big[R^{\frac{1}{2}}y\partial^{2}_{tx}a(t,x_{0}^{t},\xi_{0}^{t})-(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\eta)(\partial^{2}_{t\xi}a)(t,x_{0}^{t},\xi_{0}^{t})\big]^{w}v\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim R^{-1}(\|(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)v\|_{2}+\|(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y})v\|_{2}).

The second line in the expansion is estimated like
(A)
. Similarly, for the third line in the expansion we can argue like for the third and fourth term in the first line. For the penultimate line we write by the mean value theorem:

\begin{split}&\quad\partial_{\eta}a(t,x_{0}^{t}+y,\xi_{0}^{t}+\eta)-\partial_{\eta}a(t,x_{0}^{t},\xi_{0}^{t})\\
&=\int_{0}^{1}\partial^{2}_{x\eta}a(t,x_{0}^{t}+sy,\xi_{0}^{t}+s\eta)\cdot y+\partial^{2}_{\eta\eta}a(t,x_{0}^{t}+sy,\xi_{0}^{t}+s\eta)\cdot\eta ds.\end{split}

Then we change to standard quantization and argue like above by symbol composition

\begin{split}&\quad\|R^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial_{\eta}a(t,x_{0}^{t}+y,\xi_{0}^{t}+\eta)-\partial_{\eta}a(t,x_{0}^{t},\xi_{0}^{t})(a_{x}(t,x_{0}^{t},\xi_{0}^{t})))^{w}v\|_{2}\\
&\lesssim R^{-1}(\|R^{-\frac{1}{2}}yv\|_{2}+\|R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y}v\|_{2}+\|v\|_{2}).\end{split}

The last line in the expansion (
4.92
) can be estimated directly again.

Coming back to (
4.91
), the commutator terms are apparently better behaved. We find that after expanding the right hand side

\begin{split}&\quad R^{\frac{1}{2}}\|((R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)^{\alpha}(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y})^{\beta}(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{t})^{\gamma-1}a_{2}^{w}v\|_{2}\\
&=R^{\frac{1}{2}}\|a_{2}^{w}((R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)^{\alpha}(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y})^{\beta}(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{t})^{\gamma-1}v\|_{2}+\text{l.o.t.}\end{split}

We use the expansion for
a_{2}^{w}
like in case
\gamma=1
,
\alpha=\beta=0
to find

\begin{split}&\quad R^{\frac{1}{2}}\|a_{2}^{w}((R^{-\frac{1}{2}}y)^{\alpha}(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y})^{\beta}(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{t})^{\gamma-1}v\|_{2}\\
&\lesssim\sum_{|\alpha^{\prime}|+|\beta^{\prime}|\leq|\alpha|+|\beta|+2}\|(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y})^{\alpha^{\prime}}(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y})^{\beta^{\prime}}(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{t})^{\gamma-1}v\|_{2}\end{split}

and can now conclude the reduction (
4.90
) by induction on
\gamma
. With (
4.90
) at hand, the claim (
4.81
) follows from (
4.82
).
∎

4.3.
Localization properties of wave packets

By the regularity properties of the kernel we can now state the localization properties of the wave packets.
Recall that we let

u_{x_{0},\xi_{0}}(t,y)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}K_{x_{0},\xi_{0}}(t,y,0,\tilde{y})u_{0}(\tilde{y})d\tilde{y}

with

\begin{split}K_{x_{0},\xi_{0}}(t,y,0,\tilde{y})&=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}e^{-\frac{\rho}{2}(\tilde{y}-x)^{2}}e^{-i\xi(\tilde{y}-x)}e^{i\xi^{t}(y-x^{t})}e^{i(\psi(t,x,\xi)-\psi(s,x,\xi))}\psi_{x_{0},\xi_{0}}(x,\xi)\\
&\quad\quad\times G(t,s,x,\xi,y)dxd\xi.\end{split}

Lemma 4.13
.

The following estimate holds for any
N\geq 1
:

|u_{x_{0},\xi_{0}}(t,y)|\lesssim_{N}R^{-\frac{d}{4}}(1+R^{-\frac{1}{2}}|x_{0}^{t}-y|)^{-N}\big(\int|\psi_{(x_{0},\xi_{0})}(x,\xi)T_{r}(x_{,}\xi)|^{2}dxd\xi\big)^{\frac{1}{2}},

where
\mathcal{M}
denotes the Hardy-Littlewood maximal function.

Proof.

By the regularity properties of
G
we find the estimate

(1+R^{-\frac{1}{2}}|y-x^{t}|)^{N}|G(t,s,x,\xi,y)|\lesssim_{N}C_{R}^{2}.

This follows from Sobolev embedding, the frequency localization of
G
, and its regularity properties shown in Proposition
4.3
. This implies

|S(t,0)\phi_{x,\xi}(y)|\lesssim(1+R^{-\frac{1}{2}}|x^{t}-y|)^{-N}.

By the localization property of
\psi_{x_{0},\xi_{0}}
we can estimate

\begin{split}|u_{(x_{0},\xi_{0})}(y)|&\leq C_{R}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}|S(t,0)\phi_{(x,\xi)}(y)\psi_{(x_{0},\xi_{0})}(x,\xi)T_{R}u_{0}(x,\xi)|dxd\xi\\
&\lesssim C_{R}\int(1+R^{-\frac{1}{2}}|x^{t}-y|)^{-N}\psi_{(x_{0},\xi_{0})}(x,\xi)|T_{R}u_{0}|(x,\xi)dxd\xi\\
&\lesssim R^{-\frac{d}{4}}(1+R^{-\frac{1}{2}}|x_{0}^{t}-y|)^{-N}\|\psi_{(x_{0},\xi_{0})}T_{R}u_{0}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

∎

In the following let
\chi:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
denote a radially decreasing bump function with
\chi\equiv 1
on
B(0,1)
and
\text{supp}(\chi)\subseteq B(0,2)
. Denote
1-\chi=\chi_{\geq 1}
. We have the following spatial localization.

Corollary 4.14

(Spatial localization of wave packets)
.

Let
\delta>0
. The following holds:

u_{x_{0},\xi_{0}}(t,y)=\chi(R^{-\frac{1}{2}-\delta}(x_{0}^{t}-y))u_{x_{0},\xi_{0}}+\text{RapDec}(R)\|u_{0}\|_{2}.

We show the following regarding frequency localization:

Lemma 4.15
.

Let
\hat{u}_{x_{0},\xi_{0}}(\eta,t)=\mathcal{F}_{y}\big[u_{x_{0},\xi_{0}}(t,y)\big](\eta)
. The following localization of the spatial Fourier transform holds:

(4.93)

|\hat{u}_{x_{0},\xi_{0}}(\eta,t)|\lesssim_{N}R^{\frac{d}{4}}(1+R^{\frac{1}{2}}|\xi_{0}^{t}-\eta|)^{-N}\big(\int|\psi_{(x_{0},\xi_{0})}|^{2}|T_{r}u_{0}(x,\xi)|^{2}dxd\xi\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Proof.

We write the Fourier transform by Fubini’s theorem as

\begin{split}&\mathcal{F}_{y}\big[u_{x_{0},\xi_{0}}(y,t)\big](\eta)\\
&=C_{R}\iint\int S(t,0)\phi_{x,\xi}(\tilde{y})e^{-i\eta\tilde{y}}d\tilde{y}\psi_{(x_{0},\xi_{0})}(x,\xi)T_{R}u_{0}(x,\xi)dxd\xi.\end{split}

By the regularity properties of
G
we find

(4.94)

|\widehat{S(t,0)\phi_{x,\xi}}(\eta)|\lesssim R^{\frac{d}{2}}(1+R^{\frac{1}{2}}|\xi^{t}-\eta|)^{-N}.

With (
4.94
) at disposal, we can plug this into the preceding display and conclude (
4.93
) like in the previous lemma.

First, we handle the case
|\xi_{0}^{t}-\eta|\lesssim R^{-\frac{1}{2}}
.
The estimate is immediate from the pointwise estimate of
G
and localization to a
R^{\frac{1}{2}}
-ball such that

\big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}dye^{i(\xi^{t}-\eta)y}e^{-i\xi^{t}\cdot x^{t}}G(t,s,x,\xi,y)\big|\lesssim R^{\frac{d}{2}}C_{R}^{2}.

Consequently,

\begin{split}&\big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}dxd\xi e^{-\frac{\rho}{2}(\tilde{y}-x)^{2}}e^{-i\xi(\tilde{y}-x)}\psi_{x_{0},\xi_{0}}(x,\xi)\\
&\quad\times\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}dye^{i(\xi^{t}-\eta)y}e^{-i\xi^{t}\cdot x^{t}}G(t,s,x,\xi,y)\big|\lesssim e^{-\frac{\rho}{2}(\tilde{y}-x_{0})^{2}}\end{split}

as the integration over
(x,\xi)
yields a factor of unit size by the localization of
\psi_{x_{0},\xi_{0}}
.

Next, we turn to the case
|\xi_{0}^{t}-\eta|\gg R^{-\frac{1}{2}}
. In this case we can integrate by parts in
y
. Let
j
denote the index such that
|\xi^{t}-\eta|\sim|(\xi^{t}-\eta)_{j}|
. We find

\begin{split}&\quad\big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{i(\xi^{t}-\eta)\cdot y}e^{-i\xi^{t}\cdot x^{t}}G(t,s,x,\xi,y)dy\big|\\
&=\big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big[i(\xi^{t}-\eta)_{j}\big]^{-N}\partial_{j}^{N}e^{i(\xi^{t}-\eta)\cdot y}e^{-i\xi^{t}\cdot x^{t}}G(t,s,x,\xi,y)dy\big|\\
&=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\big|\frac{R^{-\frac{N}{2}}}{\big[i(\xi^{t}-\eta)_{j}\big]^{N}}e^{i(\xi^{t}-\eta)\cdot y}e^{-i\xi^{t}\cdot x^{t}}(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{y_{j}})^{N}G(t,s,x,\xi,y)\big|dy\\
&\lesssim\frac{R^{-\frac{N}{2}}}{|\xi_{0}^{t}-\eta|^{N}}C_{R}^{2}R^{\frac{d}{2}}.\end{split}

In the final step we use the assumption
|\xi_{0}^{t}-\eta|\gg R^{-\frac{1}{2}}
, the geometry of wave packets (Lemma
2.2
), pointwise bounds and localization of the derivatives of
G
to a
R^{\frac{1}{2}}
-ball by virtue of Proposition
4.3
.
∎

We record the following corollary:

Corollary 4.16
.

Let
\delta>0
and
\hat{u}_{x_{0},\xi_{0}}(\eta,t)=\mathcal{F}_{y}\big[u_{x_{0},\xi_{0}}(t,y)\big](\eta)
. The following localization of the spatial Fourier transform holds:

(4.95)

\hat{u}_{x_{0},\xi_{0}}(\eta,t)=\chi(R^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta}(\xi_{0}^{t}-\eta))\hat{u}_{x_{0},\xi_{0}}(\eta)+\text{RapDec}(R)\|u_{0}\|_{2}.

Finally, we show the following localization of the time frequencies:

Lemma 4.17
.

The following localization holds for the windowed Fourier transform:

(4.96)

|\mathcal{F}_{t}[u_{x_{0},\xi_{0}}\chi(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t-t_{0}))](\tau)|\lesssim R^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1+R^{\frac{1}{2}}|a(x^{t_{0}}_{0},t_{0},\xi^{t_{0}}_{0})+\tau|)^{-N}\mathcal{M}u_{0}(x_{0}).

Proof.

The estimate will again be a consequence of non-stationary phase; in this case we integrate by parts in time. To this end, we linearize the Hamilton flow in time. As a consequence of Lemma
4.8
we have the expansion

\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}x^{t}&=x^{t_{0}}+(t-t_{0})\partial_{\xi}a(x^{t_{0}},t_{0},\xi^{t_{0}})+G_{x,\xi}(t),\\
\xi^{t}&=\xi^{t_{0}}+F_{x,\xi}(t),\end{array}\right.

which satisfies for
|t-t_{0}|\lesssim R^{\frac{1}{2}}
the bounds

(4.97)

|\partial_{t}^{\alpha}F_{x,\xi}(t)|\lesssim_{\alpha}R^{\frac{-|\alpha|-1}{2}},\quad|\partial_{t}^{\beta}G_{x,\xi}(t)|\lesssim_{\beta}R^{\frac{-|\beta|}{2}}.

By the Taylor expansion we can rewrite the
t
-dependent phase function as

e^{i\xi^{t}(y-x^{t})}=e^{i(\xi^{t_{0}}+F_{x,\xi}(t))(y-x^{t})}=e^{i\xi^{t_{0}}(y-x^{t})}e^{iF_{x,\xi}(t)(y-x^{t})}.

We record the following estimate, which is a consequence of the time regularity of the Hamilton flow (Lemma
4.8
) and the Leibniz rule:

(4.98)

|\partial_{t}^{\alpha}(F_{x,\xi}(t)(y-x^{t}))|\lesssim_{\alpha}R^{-\frac{|\alpha|}{2}}(1+R^{-\frac{1}{2}}|y-x^{t}|)

provided that
\alpha\geq 0
and
|t-t_{0}|\lesssim R^{\frac{1}{2}}
. Indeed, this follows from distributing the time derivatives and (
4.97
). We continue by writing

\begin{split}&\quad e^{i\xi^{t_{0}}(y-x^{t_{0}})-i\xi^{t_{0}}((t-t_{0})\partial_{\xi}a(x^{t_{0}},\xi^{t_{0}})-G_{x,\xi}(t))}\\
&=e^{i\xi^{t_{0}}(y-x^{t_{0}})}e^{-i(t-t_{0})a(x^{t_{0}},\xi^{t_{0}})}e^{i\xi^{t_{0}}G_{x,\xi}(t)}.\end{split}

For the last term it is easy to see by (
4.97
) that

(4.99)

\big|\partial_{t}^{\alpha}(e^{i\xi^{t_{0}}G_{x,\xi}(t)})\big|\lesssim R^{-\frac{|\alpha|}{2}}\text{ for }|t-t_{0}|\lesssim R^{\frac{1}{2}}.

We obtain for the windowed Fourier transform by Fubini’s theorem:

\begin{split}&\quad\mathcal{F}_{t}[\chi(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t-t_{0}))u_{x_{0},\xi_{0}}(t,y)]\\
&=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}d\tilde{y}u_{0}(\tilde{y})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}dxd\xi e^{-\frac{\rho}{2}(\tilde{y}-x)^{2}}e^{-i\xi(\tilde{y}-x)}\psi_{x_{0},\xi_{0}}(x,\xi)\\
&\quad\quad\times\int_{\mathbb{R}}dt\chi(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t-t_{0}))e^{-it\tau}e^{i\xi^{t}(y-x^{t})}G(t,s,x,\xi,y).\end{split}

We rewrite the time-integral in the second line by the expansion of the Hamilton flow as

\begin{split}&\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}}dt\chi(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t-t_{0}))e^{-it\tau}e^{i\xi^{t}(y-x^{t})}G(t,s,x,\xi,y)\\
&=e^{i\xi^{t_{0}}(y-x^{t_{0}})}e^{it_{0}a(x^{t_{0}},\lambda\xi^{t_{0}})}\int_{\mathbb{R}}dt\chi(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t-t_{0}))e^{-it(a(x^{t_{0}},\xi^{t_{0}})+\tau)}\\
&\quad\quad\times e^{i\xi^{t_{0}}G_{x,\xi}(t)}e^{iF_{x,\xi}(t)(y-x^{t})}G(t,s,x,y).\end{split}

By Taylor expansion and Lemma
2.2
we see that for
(x,\xi)\in\text{supp}(\psi_{x_{0},\xi_{0}})
and
|\tau+a(x_{0}^{t_{0}},t_{0},\xi_{0}^{t_{0}})|\lesssim R^{-\frac{1}{2}}
that

|\tau+a(x^{t_{0}},t_{0},\xi^{t_{0}})|\lesssim R^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

In this case we can use the size of
|G|\lesssim R^{-\frac{d}{2}}
to estimate

\begin{split}|\mathcal{F}_{t}[\chi(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t-t_{0}))u_{x_{0},\xi_{0}}(t,y)]|&\lesssim R^{\frac{1}{2}}R^{-\frac{d}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}d\tilde{y}u_{0}(\tilde{y})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}e^{-\frac{\rho}{2}(\tilde{y}-x)^{2}}\psi_{x_{0},\xi_{0}}(x,\xi)\\
&\lesssim R^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{M}u_{0}(x_{0}).\end{split}

On the other hand, for
|\tau+a(x_{0}^{t_{0}},t_{0},\xi_{0}^{t_{0}})|\gg R^{-\frac{1}{2}}
we have by Lemma
2.2
that

|\tau+a(x^{t_{0}},t_{0},\xi^{t_{0}})|\gg R^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

We suppress the explicit dependence on
a
in the following to ease notation.
The above allows us to rewrite the
t
-integral by integration by parts as

\begin{split}&\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}}dt\chi(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t-t_{0}))\partial_{t}^{\alpha}\big(\frac{e^{-it(a(x^{t_{0}},\xi^{t_{0}})+\tau)}}{[-i(a(x^{t_{0}},\xi^{t_{0}})+\tau)]^{\alpha}}\big)e^{i\xi^{t_{0}}G_{x,\xi}(t)}e^{iF_{x,\xi}(t)(y-x^{t})}G(t,x,\xi,y)\\
&=(-1)^{\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{R}}dt\frac{e^{-it(a(x^{t_{0}},\xi^{t_{0}})+\tau)}}{[-i(a(x^{t_{0}},\xi^{t_{0}})+\tau)]^{\alpha}}\\
&\quad\partial_{t}^{\alpha}\big(\chi(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t-t_{0}))e^{i\xi^{t_{0}}G_{x,\xi}(t)}e^{iF_{x,\xi}(t)(y-x^{t})}G(t,s,x,\xi,y)\big).\end{split}

By the time regularity of
G
and (
4.98
) and (
4.99
), we obtain the pointwise estimate

|\partial_{t}^{\alpha}\big(\chi(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t-t_{0}))e^{i\xi^{t_{0}}G_{x,\xi}(t)}e^{iF_{x,\xi}(t)(y-x^{t})}G(t,x,\xi,y)\big)|\lesssim R^{-\frac{|\alpha|}{2}}R^{-\frac{d}{2}}\tilde{\chi}(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t-t_{0}))

for
\tilde{\chi}
a mild enlargement of
\chi
. We can conclude

\begin{split}&\quad|\mathcal{F}_{t}[\chi(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t-t_{0}))u_{x_{0},\xi_{0}}(t,y)](\tau)|\\
&\lesssim_{\alpha}R^{\frac{1}{2}}R^{-\frac{d}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}d\tilde{y}|u_{0}(\tilde{y})|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}e^{-\frac{\rho}{2}(\tilde{y}-x)^{2}}\psi_{x_{0},\xi_{0}}(x,\xi)(1+R^{\frac{1}{2}}|a(x^{t_{0}}_{0},\xi^{t_{0}}_{0})|)^{-|\alpha|}\\
&\lesssim_{\alpha}R^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+R^{\frac{1}{2}}|a(x^{t_{0}}_{0},\xi^{t_{0}}_{0})|)^{-|\alpha|}\mathcal{M}u_{0}(x_{0}).\end{split}

∎

Corollary 4.18
.

Let
\delta>0
. The following localization of the localized Fourier transform in time
\tilde{u}_{x_{0},\xi_{0}}(\tau)=\mathcal{F}_{t}[u_{x_{0},\xi_{0}}\chi(R^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t-t_{0}))](\tau)
holds:

(4.100)

|\tilde{u}_{x_{0},\xi_{0}}(\tau)|\lesssim\chi(R^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta}(\tau+a(x_{0}^{t_{0}},\xi_{0}^{t_{0}}))\tilde{u}_{x_{0},\xi_{0}}(\tau)+\text{RapDec}(R)\|u_{0}\|_{2}.

Let
\alpha_{(x_{0},\xi_{0})}=\|\psi_{(x_{0},\xi_{0})}T_{r}u_{0}\|_{L^{2}(T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{d})}
. We obtain normalized wave packets letting
\phi_{(x_{0},\xi_{0})}=u_{(x_{0},\xi_{0})}/\alpha_{(x_{0},\xi_{0})}
and in conclusion,

u=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x_{0},\xi_{0})\in\Lambda_{r},\\
\alpha_{(x_{0},\xi_{0})\neq 0}\end{subarray}}\alpha_{(x_{0},\xi_{0})}\phi_{(x_{0},\xi_{0})}

satisfies the wave packet axioms from Assumption
2.5
. The localization properties were checked above, the property

\sum_{(x_{0},\xi_{0})\in\Lambda_{r}}|\alpha_{(x_{0},\xi_{0})}|^{2}\lesssim\|u_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

is a consequence of the isometry of
T_{r}
.

As a further consequence we obtain for
r_{1}\leq r
the pointwise estimates in phase space:

|T_{r_{1}}\phi_{T}(x,\xi)|\lesssim(1+r^{-\frac{1}{2}}|x_{T}-x|+r_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}|\xi_{T}-\xi|)^{-N},

Conclusion of the Proof of Theorem
1.2
.

We need to define the pseudo-differential operators
\chi_{\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r}}(x,D)
, for which the wave packet expansion holds. Recall the thickenings of
\mathcal{Y}
given in Definition
2.4
. We define the symbol
\chi_{\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r}}
as identically one on
\mathcal{Y}_{r}
, which is the
(Rr^{-\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{0}},r^{-\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{0}})
-thickening on
\mathcal{Y}_{\nu^{-2}}
and vanishing off
\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r}
, that is the
(CRr^{-\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{0}},Cr^{-\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{0}})
-thickening.

The
L^{2}
-boundedness is a consequence of scaling: the symbols satisfy the estimates

|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}\chi_{r}(x,\xi)|\lesssim_{\alpha,\beta}(Rr^{-\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{0}})^{-|\alpha|}r^{|\beta|(\frac{1}{2}-\delta_{0})}.

Consequently, we obtain by change of variables
\xi=r^{-\frac{1}{2}}\xi^{\prime}
and
x=r^{\frac{1}{2}}x^{\prime}

\int\big|\int\chi(x,\xi)\hat{f}(\xi)e^{ix\cdot\xi}d\xi\big|^{2}dx=r^{-\frac{d}{2}}\int\big|\int\chi(r^{\frac{1}{2}}x^{\prime},r^{-\frac{1}{2}}\xi^{\prime})\hat{f}(r^{-\frac{1}{2}}\xi^{\prime})e^{ix^{\prime}\cdot\xi^{\prime}}d\xi^{\prime}\big|^{2}dx^{\prime}.

The resulting symbol is in
S_{0,0}^{0}
. Indeed, we have

|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}\chi(r^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta_{0}}x,r^{-\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{0}}\xi)|\lesssim_{\alpha,\beta}(Rr^{-1+\delta_{0}})^{-|\alpha|}r^{-\delta_{0}|\beta|}.

It follows from the Calderon–Vaillancourt estimate

\int\big|\int\chi(r^{\frac{1}{2}}x^{\prime},r^{-\frac{1}{2}}\xi^{\prime})\hat{f}(r^{-\frac{1}{2}}\xi^{\prime})e^{ix^{\prime}\cdot\xi^{\prime}}d\xi^{\prime}\big|^{2}dx^{\prime}\lesssim\int|\hat{f}(r^{-\frac{1}{2}}\xi^{\prime})|^{2}d\xi^{\prime},

which yields the claim.

We turn to establishing the wave packet decomposition by separating the contribution from
\overline{\mathcal{Y}}
and
\overline{\mathcal{Y}}^{c}
:

\begin{split}&\quad S(t,0)T_{r}^{*}T_{r}(\chi_{\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r}}(x,D)f)\\
&=S(t,0)T_{r}^{*}\big(\sum_{(x_{0},\xi_{0})\in\Lambda_{r}\cap\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r}}\psi_{x_{0},\xi_{0}}+\sum_{(x_{0},\xi_{0})\notin\Lambda_{r}\cap\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r}^{c}}\psi_{x_{0},\xi_{0}}\big)T_{r}(\chi_{\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r}}(x,D)f).\end{split}

Recall that
\psi_{x_{0},\xi_{0}}
is a smooth bump function adapted to
B(x_{0},r^{\frac{1}{2}})\times B(\xi_{0},r^{-\frac{1}{2}})
.

The first sum gives rise to the wave packet decomposition

\begin{split}&\quad S(t,0)T_{r}^{*}\sum_{(x_{0},\xi_{0})\in\Lambda_{r}\cap\overline{\mathcal{Y}}}\psi_{x_{0},\xi_{0}}(T_{r}\chi_{\mathcal{Y}}(x,D)f)\\
&=\sum_{(x_{0},\xi_{0})\in\Lambda_{r}\cap\overline{\mathcal{Y}}}\int(S(t,0)\phi_{x,\xi})\psi_{x_{0},\xi_{0}}T_{r}(\chi_{\mathcal{Y}}(x,D)f)(x,\xi)dxd\xi,\end{split}

which has been analyzed in Section
4.3
. The veracity of the wave packet axioms in Assumption
2.5
follows from Corollaries
4.14
,
4.16
, and
4.18
.

It remains to prove the error estimate for the second term. First, we handle the claimed
L^{2}
-estimate. In the following we apply the
L^{2}
-boundedness of the phase space transform and the
L^{2}
-boundedness of the evolution to find

\begin{split}&\;\|S(t,0)T_{r}^{*}\sum_{(x_{0},\xi_{0})\notin\Lambda_{r}\cap\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r}^{c}}\psi_{x_{0},\xi_{0}}T_{r}(\chi_{\mathcal{Y}}(x,D)f)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
&\lesssim\big\|\sum_{(x_{0},\xi_{0})\notin\Lambda_{r}\cap\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r}^{c}}\psi_{x_{0},\xi_{0}}T_{r}(\chi_{\mathcal{Y}}(x,D)f)\big\|_{L^{2}(T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{d})}.\end{split}

The reason why we can show a favorable estimate is that the support of
\chi_{\mathcal{Y}}
and
\text{supp}(\sum_{(x_{0},\xi_{0})\notin\Lambda_{r}\cap\overline{\mathcal{Y}}^{c}}\psi_{(x_{0},\xi_{0})})
are separated by
CRr^{-\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{0}}
in space and
Cr^{-\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{0}}
in frequency when we apply the FBI transform on a finer scale. This will be used in the following.

We recall the conjugation of pseudo-differential operators (see
[
36
, p. 351]
) with the FBI transform given by

T_{\lambda}f(z)=\lambda^{\frac{3d}{4}}2^{-\frac{d}{2}}\pi^{-\frac{3d}{4}}\int e^{-\frac{\lambda}{2}(z-y)^{2}}f(y)dy,\quad z=x-i\xi\in T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{d}.

For a compactly supported symbol let
a_{\lambda}(x,\xi)=a(x,\xi/\lambda)
be a rescaling supported at frequencies
\lambda
. By formally conjugating position and momentum operator we obtain the formal asymptotic

T_{\lambda}A_{\lambda}(x,D)\approx\sum_{\alpha,\beta}(\partial_{\xi}-\lambda\xi)^{\alpha}\frac{\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}a(x,\xi)}{\alpha!\beta!(-i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}\lambda^{|\beta|}}(\frac{1}{i}\partial_{x}-\lambda\xi)^{\beta}T_{\lambda}.

For symbols with uniformly bounded
\xi
-derivatives we have error estimates in terms of the
C^{s}
-norm for conjugation with the truncated series defined by

\tilde{a}^{s}_{\lambda}=\sum_{|\alpha|+|\beta|<s}(\partial_{\xi}-\lambda\xi)^{\alpha}\frac{\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}a(x,\xi)}{\alpha!\beta!(-i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}\lambda^{|\beta|}}\big(\frac{1}{i}\partial_{x}-\lambda\xi\big)^{\beta}.

It holds for the remainder
R^{s}_{\lambda,a}=T_{\lambda}A_{\lambda}-\tilde{a}^{s}_{\lambda}T_{\lambda}
.

Theorem 4.19

(
[
37
, Theorem 1]
)
.

Assume that
a\in C^{s}_{x}C^{\infty}_{c}
. Then

\|R^{s}_{\lambda,a}\|_{L^{2}\to L^{2}_{\Phi}}\leq c\lambda^{-\frac{s}{2}}.

We aim to apply the theorem at scale
\lambda=r^{\frac{1}{2}}
. We can change from the currently used phase space transform
T_{r}
to
T_{\lambda}
by the change of variables
\xi=r^{-\frac{1}{2}}\xi^{\prime}
and
x=r^{\frac{1}{2}}x^{\prime}
. The
C^{s}_{x}
-bounds of
a(x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})=\chi_{\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r}}(r^{\frac{1}{2}}x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})
are decaying in
s
.

Next, since the support of
\sum_{(x_{0},\xi_{0})\notin\Lambda_{r}\cap\overline{\mathcal{Y}_{r}}}
and
\chi_{\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r}}
are disjoint, the corresponding conjugated series does not contribute for any
s
. A word of clarification here: the derivatives of
a
in
\xi
are not uniformly bounded. However, the expressions in the series improve also with derivatives in
\xi
. Truncating the series high enough remedies for the lack of uniform boundedness in
C^{\infty}_{c}
. It is also possible to do things directly and prove a kernel estimate for
\chi_{\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r}}(x,D)T_{r}
by integration by parts. Here we preferred to relate to the existing literature.

We find from the error term in Theorem
4.19
:

\big\|\sum_{(x_{0},\xi_{0})\notin\Lambda_{r}\cap\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r}^{c}\psi_{(x_{0},\xi_{0})}}T_{r}(\chi_{\mathcal{Y}}(x,D)f)\|_{L^{2}(T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim_{\delta}\text{RapDec}(r)\|f\|_{L^{2}}.

This finishes the proof.

∎

4.4.
Wave packet decompositions at different scales and Assumption
2.8

With the basic wave packet decomposition established in the preceding subsection we comment on the frame property for approximate solutions in Assumption
2.8
. Recall that we propose the existence of a family of nested sets
(\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r,t})_{r\in[\nu^{-2-\delta_{0}},R]}
at times
t\in[-R,R]
:

\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{R,t}\subseteq\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r_{2},t}\subseteq\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r_{1},t}\subseteq\overline{\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq T^{*}\mathbb{R}^{d}

with
R\geq r_{2}\geq r_{1}\geq\nu^{-2-\delta_{0}}
and associated PDOs
\chi_{\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r,t}}
.

For
t=0
we have explained the sets
\mathcal{Y}_{r}
,
\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r}
and
\chi_{\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r}}
in the preceding paragraph. The sets
\mathcal{Y}_{r}
are transported along the flow resulting in the sets
\mathcal{Y}_{r,t}
and we consider again the margins
C(R^{-\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{0}},r^{-\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{0}})
of
\mathcal{Y}_{r,t}
resulting in the sets
\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r,t}
.

(
2.11
) then follows from Hamiltonians
p
which satisfy Assumption
4.1
from Theorem
1.2
and finite speed of propagation to limit the number of tubes to a power of
r
. More precisely, for given
Q_{r}
we can further localize
\chi_{\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r,t_{i}}}(x,D)f
to a cube
Q_{r,x}
of size
Cr
such that

S(t,t_{i})\chi_{\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r,t_{i}}}f=S(t,t_{i})[\chi_{Q_{r,x}}\chi_{\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r,t_{i}}}f]+g(t)

such that

\|g\|_{L^{p}_{x,t}(Q_{r})}\lesssim\nu^{d\big(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}\big)}\text{RapDec}(r)\|f\|_{L^{2}}.

For
\chi_{Q_{r,x}}\chi_{\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r,t_{i}}}f
we carry out the wave packet construction from the previous paragraph, noting that
\chi_{Q_{r,x}}
does not essentially change the frequency support of
\chi_{\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r,t_{i}}}
and taking into account the additional spatial localization allows us to choose
\#\mathbb{T}\lesssim r^{100d}
.

The relevance of considering nested sets for different scales
r_{1}\leq r_{2}^{1-\delta}
is that upon evolving the essential part as
S(t_{f},t_{i})\chi_{\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r_{2},t_{i}}}f=\sum_{T\in\mathbb{T}_{r_{2}}\subseteq\Lambda_{r_{2}}\cap\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r_{2},t_{i}}}a_{T}\phi_{T}+g(t)
for
|t_{i}-t_{f}|\leq r_{2}
the final state at
t_{f}
can be regarded as

(4.101)

\sum_{T\in\mathbb{T}_{r_{2}}\subseteq\Lambda_{r_{2}}\cap\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r_{2},t_{i}}}a_{T}\phi_{T}(t_{f})=\chi_{\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r_{1},t_{f}}}\sum_{T\in\mathbb{T}_{r_{2}}\subseteq\Lambda_{r_{2}}\cap\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r_{2},t}}a_{T}\phi_{T}+\text{RapDec}(r_{1})\|\chi_{\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r_{2},t_{i}}}f\|_{2}.

This will allow us to regard the solution on a smaller space-time scale as localized on a coarser scale. This is required in the proofs of Theorems
2.13
and
2.16
to employ the induction hypothesis.

We turn to the proof of (
2.13
), that is for
\phi_{T}
a normalized wave packet at scale
r_{2}
, having the localization

(4.102)

\|(1-\chi_{\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r_{1},t_{f}}}(x,D))\phi_{T}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{\delta}\text{RapDec}(r_{1})

provided that
(x_{T}(t),\xi_{T}(t))\in\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r_{2},t_{f}}
and
r_{1}\leq r_{2}^{1-\delta}
.
First recall that
\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r_{1},t_{f}}
is the
C(Rr_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{0}},r_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{0}})
-thickening and we have the phase space localization

|T_{r_{1}}\phi_{T}(t,x,\xi)|\lesssim_{N}(1+r_{2}^{-\frac{1}{2}}|x_{T}(t)-x|+r^{\frac{1}{2}}_{1}|\xi_{T}(t)-\xi|)^{-N}.

Since
r_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\ll Rr_{2}^{-\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{0}}\ll Rr_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{0}}
, and
r_{2}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\ll r_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{0}}
, the essential support of
T_{r_{1}}\phi_{T}
is separated by a margin of
(r_{2}^{\delta},r_{1}^{-\delta})
and the rapid decay (
4.102
) follows from conjugating
(1-\chi_{\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r_{1},t_{f}}})
with
T_{r_{1}}
to phase space like in the previous paragraph. We omit the details to avoid repetition.

Finally, we observe that evolving
\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r_{1},t_{i}}
under
p
yields a thickening of size

C(Rr_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{0}},r_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{0}})
of
\mathcal{Y}_{r_{1},t_{f}}
by the bi-Lipschitz property of the flow, Lemma
2.2
.
Consequently, for
\phi_{T}
a wave packet with
T\in\Lambda_{r_{1}}\cap\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r_{1},t_{i}}
we have
(x_{T}(t_{f}),\xi_{T}(t_{f}))\in\mathcal{Y}_{r_{2},t_{f}}
and can arrange for the support of
1-\chi_{\overline{\mathcal{Y}}_{r_{1},t_{f}}}
to be separated by a margin
C(Rr_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{0}},r_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{0}})
.

5.
Applications to linear and bilinear estimates

In this section we show how the wave packet decomposition can be utilized to prove Strichartz and bilinear estimates. In the Appendix we show in Propositions
A.2
and
A.5
how to use wave packet decompositions and non-degeneracy properties of the Hamiltonian flow to obtain dispersive estimates.

5.1.
Estimates for wave equations with rough coefficients

We focus on wave equations governed by a time-independent metric with coefficients
g^{ij}\in C^{1,s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
s\in[0,1]
for definiteness. The ellipticity condition reads

(5.103)

\exists\lambda,\Lambda>0:\,\forall(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}^{d+1}:\;\forall\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\;\lambda|\xi|^{2}\leq g^{ij}(t,x)\xi_{i}\xi_{j}\leq\Lambda|\xi|^{2}.

The Cauchy problem for the wave equation is given by

(5.104)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\partial_{t}^{2}u&=\partial_{i}g^{ij}\partial_{j}u,\quad(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d},\\
u(0)&=u_{0},\quad\dot{u}(0)=u_{1}.\end{array}\right.

First, we outline the proof of Strichartz estimates. Here we recover the sharp estimates due to Tataru
[
37
]
. The key argument to descend from
C^{1,1}
-coefficients to lower regularity is as before (
[
1
,
37
,
38
]
) a paradifferential decomposition for the coefficients of the metric:

For
C^{1,1}
-coefficients, and solutions at frequency
N
, aa frequency truncation of the metric coefficients ar frequency
N^{\frac{1}{3}}

allows us to obtain a
C^{1,1}
-wave packet decomposition on the unit time scale with an error term that can be treated perturbatively invoking Duhamel’s formula. In turn, is well-known that a wave packet decomposition, which satisfies
C^{1,1}
-regularity bounds, can be used to recover Euclidean Strichartz estimates (e.g. Smith
[
28
]
, Smith–Tataru
[
30
]
, Candy–Herr
[
13
]
).

For rougher coefficients, when attempting the same paradifferential decomposition the contribution from the Duhamel term becomes large.
We may truncate the coefficients at higher frequencies to ameliorate its contribution.
But when truncating the coefficients at frequencies
N^{\sigma}
,
\sigma>\frac{1}{2}
, we can only obtain a wave packet decomposition on a frequency-dependent time scale, and a loss is incurred when summing over the frequency-dependent time intervals. Our choice of
\sigma
is dictated by balancing these two contributions.

Since we obtain the sharp results due to Tataru
[
37
]
, this shows that our regularity requirements on the wave packet decomposition are sharp in the
C^{s}
-scale.

Secondly, we show Theorem
1.1
, which is concerned with bilinear estimates. The proof uses similar reductions and decompositions like in the linear case: after recovering the wave packet decomposition on a frequency-dependent time scale, we can use the wave packet decomposition and invoke Theorem
2.16
.

5.1.1.
Strichartz estimates

In the following we recover
[
37
, Theorem 4]
; we consider only
d\geq 4
because the endpoint Strichartz space
L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{\frac{2(d-1)}{d-3}}
becomes admissible and moreover, in order to simplify the exposition, we assume that the coefficients are time-independent. Sharpness of the result was proved by Smith–Tataru
[
29
]
.

Theorem 5.1

(Endpoint Strichartz estimates via wave packet decompositions)
.

Let
s\in[0,1]
, and
(g^{ij})_{ij}\subseteq C^{1,s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, which satisfies (
5.103
) and
\|g^{ij}\|_{C^{1,s}}\lesssim 1
.
Let
d\geq 4
,
q=\frac{2(d-1)}{d-3}
,
\kappa_{0}=\frac{d-1}{2}-\frac{d}{q}
, and
\kappa_{1}=\frac{1-s}{2(3+s)}
. The following estimate for solutions to (
5.104
) holds:

(5.105)

\|\langle D_{x}\rangle^{1-\kappa_{0}-\kappa_{1}}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}([0,1],L^{q}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\lesssim\|u_{0}\|_{H^{1}}+\|u_{1}\|_{L^{2}}.

Proof.

The initial reductions are very standard, so we shall be brief.
By finite speed of propagation, it suffices to show an estimate on
[0,1]\times B_{d}(0,1)
with
u
essentially supported on
B_{d}(0,1)
. Moreover, by a finite partition and mild dilation we can suppose that
\|g^{ij}\|_{\dot{C}^{1,s}}\ll 1
. We employ a Littlewood-Paley decomposition

u=\sum_{N\geq 1}P_{N}u=\sum_{N\geq 1}u_{N}

and carry out a paraproduct decomposition

(5.106)

\partial_{t}^{2}u_{N}=P_{N}(\partial_{i}g^{ij}_{\ll N}\partial_{j}\tilde{P}_{N}u)+P_{N}(\partial_{i}g^{ij}_{\sim N}\partial_{j}P_{\ll N}u)+P_{N}\sum_{M\gg N}\partial_{i}g^{ij}_{M}\partial_{j}u_{M}.

For the third expression we find

\|P_{N}g^{ij}_{M}\partial_{j}P_{M}u\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\|\partial_{x}g^{ij}_{M}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|P_{M}u\|_{L^{2}_{x}},

which is of lower order and can be estimated as part of the Duhamel term. Similarly, the second term in (
5.106
) can be handled. By a commutator argument we find

P_{N}(\partial_{i}g^{ij}_{\ll N}\partial_{j}\tilde{P}_{N}u)=\partial_{i}g^{ij}_{\ll N}\partial_{j}P_{N}u+\text{l.o.t.}.

In summary, by Bernstein’s inequality and the energy estimate it suffices to consider frequencies
N\gg 1
and show

(5.107)

N^{1-\kappa_{0}}\|P_{N}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}([0,1];L^{q}_{x})}\lesssim\|\nabla_{t,x}P_{N}u\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}+\|f\|_{L^{1}L^{2}}

for frequency-localized solutions to

\partial_{t}^{2}u_{N}=\partial_{i}g^{ij}_{\ll N}\partial_{j}u_{N}+f,

with
f
being a lower order term. Indeed, the propagation approximately preserves frequency-localization.

For the proof of (
5.107
) we carry out a paradifferential decomposition

\partial_{i}g^{ij}_{\ll N}\partial_{j}P_{N}u=\partial_{i}g^{ij}_{\ll N^{\sigma}}\partial_{j}P_{N}u+\partial_{i}g^{ij}_{N^{\sigma}\lesssim\cdot\ll N}\partial_{j}P_{N}u

and want to treat the second term as part of the Duhamel term.

We shall argue below that truncating the coefficients at
N^{\sigma}
allows us to use Euclidean Strichartz estimates on a time interval
I
of length
N^{1-2\sigma}
. We show how the proof is concluded with this information at hand.

Let
L_{N}=-\partial_{i}g^{ij}_{\lesssim N^{\sigma}}\partial_{j}
.
We have for the error term from truncating the coefficients at frequencies
N^{\sigma}
:

\begin{split}\big\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{L_{N}}}\partial_{i}g^{ij}_{N^{\sigma}\lesssim\cdot\ll N}\partial_{j}u_{N}\big\|_{L^{2}_{x}}&\lesssim N\|g^{ij}_{\gtrsim N^{\sigma}}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\|u_{N}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
&=N^{1-\sigma(1+s)}N^{\sigma(1+s)}\|g^{ij}_{\gtrsim N^{\sigma}}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\|u_{N}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
&\lesssim N^{1-\sigma(1+s)}\|g^{ij}\|_{C^{1,s}}\|u_{N}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}.\end{split}

Applying the Euclidean Strichartz estimates and bearing in mind the estimate for the error term, we find

\begin{split}N^{1-\kappa_{0}}\|u_{N}\|_{L_{t}^{2}(I;L_{x}^{q})}&\lesssim\|u_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}(I;L_{x}^{2})}+N^{1-\sigma(1+s)}\|u_{N}\|_{L_{t}^{1}(I;L_{x}^{2})}+\|f\|_{L_{I}^{1}L_{x}^{2}}\\
&\lesssim(1+N^{2-3\sigma-\sigma s})\|u_{N}\|_{L_{I}^{\infty}L_{x}^{2}}+\|f\|_{L_{I}^{1}L_{x}^{2}}.\end{split}

The last estimate follows from Hölder’s inequality on the interval
I
.
The contribution for the two terms is balanced for
\sigma=\frac{2}{3+s}
. Carrying out an
\ell^{2}_{I}
-sum over the frequency-dependent time intervals incurs the additional loss
N^{\kappa_{1}}
. To finish the proof, it remains to argue that we have the Euclidean Strichartz estimates on the intervals
I
.

It suffices to estimate the half-wave evolution for an operator with truncated frequencies. It is well-known (see e.g.
[
31
, Theorem 3.3.1]
) that

(5.108)

\sqrt{L_{N}}f=a^{w}(x,t;D)f+a_{0}^{w}f,\quad a(x;\xi)=(g_{\leq N^{\sigma}}^{ij}(x)\xi_{i}\xi_{j})^{\frac{1}{2}}

with
a^{w}_{0}:L^{2}\to L^{2}
uniformly bounded. Then, by a similar Duhamel argument by which we replace the coefficients with their truncated versions it suffices to show Strichartz estimates for
S^{\pm}_{N}(t)
, which denotes the propagator for
D_{t}\pm a^{w}
. After rescaling to unit frequencies
|\xi|\sim 1
, the symbol reads

a_{N}(t,x,\xi)=(g^{ij}_{\leq N^{\sigma}}(N^{-1}x)\xi_{i}\xi_{j})^{\frac{1}{2}}.

We check that the symbol regularity

(5.109)

|\partial_{z}^{\alpha}\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}a(x,t;\xi)|\lesssim_{\alpha,\beta}\begin{cases}R^{-|\alpha|},&\quad 0\leq|\alpha|\leq 2,\\
R^{-\frac{(|\alpha|-2)_{+}}{2}-2},&\quad|\alpha|\geq 2.\end{cases}

for Theorem
1.2
is satisfied for
R=N^{2(1-\sigma)}
. Hence, we recover the wave packet decomposition by Theorem
1.2
, the dispersive estimate by Proposition
A.5
, and consequently Euclidean Strichartz estimates by the Keel–Tao argument
[
20
]
on intervals of length
|I|=N^{1-2\sigma}
. The proof is complete.
∎

5.1.2.
Bilinear estimates

We turn to the proof of Theorem
1.1
, which is concerned with bilinear estimates for solutions
u_{i}
to

\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\partial_{t}^{2}u_{1}&=\partial_{i}g^{ij}_{1}\partial_{j}u_{1}+f_{1},\\
\partial_{t}^{2}u_{2}&=\partial_{i}g^{ij}_{2}\partial_{j}u_{2}+f_{2},\quad(t,x)\in[0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\end{array}\right.

with
f_{i}\in L_{t}^{1}([0,1];L_{x}^{2})
. We assume that
u_{i}
satisfy the frequency localizations

\text{supp}(\hat{u}_{i})\subseteq\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:|\xi|\sim N,\quad\big|\frac{\xi}{|\xi|}-\xi_{*}\big|\leq\varepsilon\ll 1\}.

Moreover, suppose that
g^{ij}_{k}\in C^{1,s}
,
k=1,2
,
s\in[0,1]
and
(g^{ij}_{k})_{i,j}
satisfy (
5.103
) and are frequency-truncated at frequencies
\ll N
. Moreover, we suppose that

|g^{ij}_{k}-\nu_{k}\delta_{ij}|\leq\varepsilon\ll 1

and recall that
p=p_{d}=\frac{d+3}{d+1}
.

Proof of Theorem
1.1
.

We adjust the arguments from the previous paragraph.
Like above we use the energy estimate to reduce to

(5.110)

N^{2}\|u_{1}u_{2}\|_{L^{p}_{t,x}([0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim_{\varepsilon}N^{\frac{4}{3+s}\frac{d-1}{d+3}+\varepsilon}\prod_{i=1}^{2}\big(\|\nabla_{t,x}u_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}+\|f_{i}\|_{L^{1}L^{2}}\big).

We consider equations with truncated coefficients

\partial^{2}_{t}u_{k}=\partial_{i}g^{ij}_{k,\leq N^{\sigma}}\partial_{j}u_{k}+\partial_{i}g^{ij}_{k,N^{\sigma}\lesssim\cdot\ll N}\partial_{j}u_{k}+f_{k}

with
f_{k}\in L_{t}^{1}L_{x}^{2}
. In the following it will be important that we have for the second term approximately the same frequency localization of
u_{k}
. Let
L_{N,k}=-\partial_{i}g_{k,\leq N^{\sigma}}^{ij}\partial_{j}
.

By the same argument as in the previous theorem, we may replace the half-wave propagators
e^{\pm it\sqrt{L_{N,k}}}
with
S_{N,k}^{\pm}(t)
, which is the evolution generated by
D_{t}\pm a^{w}
, for
a(x,t;\xi)=\big(g^{ij}_{k,\leq N^{\sigma}}\xi_{i}\xi_{j}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}
. We have already seen above that we obtain a wave packet decomposition on intervals of length
N^{1-2\sigma}
(after plugging in the finite speed of propagation). When estimating products

\|S_{N,1}^{\pm}g_{1}S_{N,2}^{\pm}g_{2}\|_{L^{p}_{t,x}(I\times B_{d}(x,I))}

by Theorem
2.16
we need to check the transversality assumptions on the flows, with the initial data
g_{i}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
having the frequency support assumptions from the outset. For products
S_{N,1}^{+}(t)g_{1}S^{+}_{N,2}(t)g_{2}
note that

|\nabla_{\xi}p_{1}(z,\xi^{1})-\nabla_{\xi}p_{2}(z,\xi^{2})|=\big|\big(\frac{g^{ij}_{1,\leq N^{\sigma}}\xi^{1}_{j}}{(g^{ij}_{1,\leq N^{\sigma}}\xi^{1}_{i}\xi^{1}_{j})^{\frac{1}{2}}}-\frac{g^{ij}_{2,\leq N^{\sigma}}\xi^{2}_{j}}{(g^{ij}_{2,\leq N^{\sigma}}\xi^{2}_{i}\xi^{2}_{j})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\big)\mathbf{e}_{i}\big|.

The error incurred replacing
g^{ij}_{k}
with
\nu_{k}
is
\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon)
, and similarly, when replacing
\xi^{i}
with
\xi_{*}
. Consequently,

|\nabla_{\xi}p_{1}(z,\xi^{1})-\nabla_{\xi}p_{2}(z,\xi^{2})|=\big|\frac{\nu_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\xi_{*}}{|\xi_{*}|}-\frac{\nu_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\xi_{*}}{|\xi_{*}|}\big|+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon).

We conclude the transversality estimate by noting that

\big|\nu_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}-\nu_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big|\sim|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}|\sim 1.

Next, we check the second transversality condition, which reads in the present context:

\big|\langle\xi_{*},\frac{g^{ij}_{1}\xi^{1}_{j}\mathbf{e}_{i}}{(g^{ij}_{1,\leq N^{\sigma}}\xi^{1}_{i}\xi^{1}_{j})^{\frac{1}{2}}}-\frac{g^{ij}_{2}\xi^{2}_{j}\mathbf{e}_{i}}{(g^{ij}_{2,\leq N^{\sigma}}\xi^{2}_{i}\xi^{2}_{j})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\rangle\big|\gtrsim 1.

Replacing
\xi^{i}
with
\xi_{*}
and
g^{ij}_{k,\leq N^{\sigma}}
with
\nu_{k}\delta_{ij}
incurs like above a loss of
\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon)
. We find

\big|\langle\xi_{*},\nu_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\xi_{*}}{|\xi_{*}|}-\nu_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\xi_{*}}{|\xi_{*}|}\rangle\big|\gtrsim|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}|\gtrsim 1.

Upon replacing
p_{2}\to-p_{2}
we see that the transversality expressions are actually better behaved and we can apply Theorem
2.16
also when estimating the mixed expressions
S^{+}_{N,1}(t)g_{1}
,
S^{-}_{N,2}(t)g_{2}
.

By the
L^{2}
-bound for the Duhamel term

\big\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{L_{k,N}}}\partial_{i}g^{ij}_{k,N^{\sigma}\lesssim\cdot\ll N}\partial_{j}u_{k}(t)\big\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim N^{1-\sigma(1+s)}\|u_{k}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}

we recover on time intervals
I
,
|I|=N^{1-2\sigma}
the Euclidean estimate

\begin{split}&\quad N^{2}\|u_{1}u_{2}\|_{L^{p_{d}}_{t,x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\\
&\lesssim_{\varepsilon}N^{\frac{d-1}{d+3}+\varepsilon}\prod_{i=1}^{2}\big(\|\nabla_{t,x}u_{i}\|_{{L^{\infty}}_{I}L^{2}_{x}}+N^{1-\sigma(1+s)}\|u_{i}\|_{L_{I}^{1}L_{x}^{2}}+N\|f_{i}\|_{L_{I}^{1}L_{x}^{2}}\big)\\
&\lesssim_{\varepsilon}N^{\frac{d-1}{d+3}+\varepsilon}\prod_{i=1}^{2}\big((1+N^{1-\sigma(1+s)+1-2\sigma})\|u_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}_{I}L_{x}^{2}}+N\|f_{i}\|_{L_{I}^{1}L_{x}^{2}}\big).\end{split}

Choosing
\sigma=\frac{2}{3+s}
we simplify

N^{2}\|u_{1}u_{2}\|_{L^{p_{d}}_{t,x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim_{\varepsilon}N^{\frac{d-1}{d+3}+\varepsilon}\prod_{i=1}^{2}\big(\|\nabla_{t,x}u_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}_{I}L^{2}_{x}}+N\|f_{i}\|_{L_{I}^{1}L_{x}^{2}}\big).

It remains to carry out the
\ell^{p_{d}}_{I}
-sum over intervals of length
|I|=N^{1-2\sigma}
, which yields the claimed estimate. The proof is complete.
∎

5.2.
Estimates for Schrödinger equations with rough coefficients

We consider a solution to the Schrödinger equation

(5.111)

i\partial_{t}u_{N}+\partial_{i}a^{ij}\partial_{j}u_{N}=f_{N}\in L^{1}L^{2}

on the frequency-dependent time interval
T=[0,N^{-1}]
with
a^{ij}\in C^{1}_{t}C_{x}^{1,s}
,
s\in[0,1]
, and
\text{supp}(\hat{u}_{N})\subseteq\{|\xi|\sim N\}
.

5.2.1.
Strichartz estimates

We require non-degeneracy of
a^{ij}
given by

(5.112)

\exists c,C>0:\forall\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\forall(t,x)\in R^{d+1}:c|\xi|\leq|a^{ij}(t,x)\xi_{j}|\leq C|\xi|.

Schrödinger equations with operators satisfying this condition can be classified
as either
elliptic
, where the matrix
a^{ij}
is of definite (positive or negative)
sign, or as
ultrahyperbolic
, where it is of indefinite sign.

Here we recover the semiclassical Strichartz estimates without derivative loss for
s=1
, firstly proved in the elliptic case by Staffilani–Tataru
[
32
]
, see also
[
41
]
. Burq–Gérard–Tzvetkov
[
8
]
provided a semiclassical interpretation and showed that there are compact Riemannian manifolds
(M,g)
, e.g., the sphere, for which the Strichartz estimate for the Laplace–Beltrami operator on a finite time interval

\|P_{N}e^{it\Delta_{g}}f\|_{L_{t}^{p}([0,1],L^{q}_{x}(M))}\lesssim N^{\frac{1}{p}}\|f\|_{L^{2}(M)}

cannot be improved for sharp Schrödinger admissible pairs
(p,q,d)
which satisfy

\frac{2}{p}+\frac{d}{q}=\frac{d}{2},\quad(p,q,d)\neq(2,\infty,2).

The semiclassical Strichartz estimates are completely similar in the ultrahyperbolic case, as only non-degeneracy is used in the proof, see the Appendix

For comparison recall that sharp linear Strichartz estimates on Euclidean space are given by

\|e^{it\Delta}f\|_{L_{t}^{p}(\mathbb{R};L^{q}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}}.

In the following we illustrate how our wave packet decomposition yields lossless estimates for rough coefficients on frequency-dependent time scales. The argument is similar to the above: we employ a paradifferential decomposition, which provides us with a wave packet decomposition on a smaller time scale than
N^{-1}
. We balance the error from additional decomposition in time and the error from the Duhamel term. After the parabolic rescaling, reducing to unit frequencies, the argument becomes essentially a reprise of the proof of Theorem
5.1
. The assumption
d\geq 3
is again a simplification to work with the endpoint.

Theorem 5.2

(Endpoint Strichartz estimates for rough coefficients)
.

Let
d\geq 3
,
s\in[0,1]
, and
(a^{ij})_{i,j}\in C^{0,1}_{t}C^{1,s}_{x}
be uniformly non-degenerate (
5.112
). Let
q=\frac{2d}{d-2}
and
N\gg 1
. Let
u
be a solution to

i\partial_{t}u+\partial_{i}a^{ij}_{\ll N}\partial_{j}u=f

with
\text{supp}(\hat{u})\subseteq\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:|\xi|\sim N\}
. Then the following estimate holds:

(5.113)

N^{-\kappa}\|u\|_{L_{t}^{2}([0,N^{-1}],L^{q}_{x})}\lesssim\|u(0)\|_{L^{2}}+\|f\|_{L^{1}L^{2}}

with
\kappa=\frac{1-s}{2(3+s)}
.

Proof.

We begin with a straightforward energy estimate

\|u\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}\lesssim\|u(0)\|_{L^{2}}+\|f\|_{L^{1}L^{2}}

which allows us to reduce (
5.113
) to

(5.114)

N^{-\kappa}\|u\|_{L_{t}^{2}([0,N^{-1}],L^{q}_{x})}\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}+\|f\|_{L^{1}L^{2}}

We employ a paradifferential decomposition of the coefficients
a_{ij}
at spatial frequencies
N^{\sigma}
,
\sigma\in[\frac{1}{2},1]
to be chosen depending on
s
, to
rewrite the equation for
u
in the form

i\partial_{t}u+\partial_{i}a^{ij}_{\ll N,\leq N^{\sigma}}\partial_{j}u=g:=-\partial_{i}a^{ij}_{c}\partial_{j}u+f.

Above
a^{ij}_{\ll N,\leq N^{\sigma}}
indicates that the temporal frequencies are truncated at
\ll N
and the spatial frequencies at size
N^{\sigma}
, while
a^{ij}_{c}
denotes the complement.

By finite speed of propagation on
[0,N^{-1}]
, we can confine
u
spatially to a unit ball. We carry out the estimate on
[0,N^{-1}]\times B_{d}(0,1)
by rescaling

t\to N^{2}t,\qquad x\to Nx,\qquad\xi\to\xi/N,\qquad u_{N}(t,x)=N^{-\frac{d}{2}}u(N^{-1}x,N^{-2}t),

noting that the the Strichartz norms rest unchanged

\|u\|_{L_{t}^{2}([0,N^{-1}],L^{q}_{x}(B_{d}(0,1))}=\|u_{N}\|_{L_{t}^{2}([0,N],L^{q}_{x}(B_{d}(0,N))}.

and so do the energy norms, therefore the bound (
5.114
) also remains the same.

The functions
u_{N}
are now localized at spatial frequency
1
, and the rescaled symbols are given by

a(x,t;\xi)=a_{\ll N,\leq N^{\sigma}}^{ij}(N^{-1}x,N^{-2}t)\xi_{i}\xi_{j}.

For an initial regularity
a^{ij}\in C^{0,1}_{t}C^{1,s}_{x}
we note that (
5.109
) is satisfied for
R=N^{2-2\sigma}
. Like above the rescaled time interval
[0,N]
has to be split into
N^{2\sigma-1}
intervals of length
R
. On the small intervals we can use the wave packet decomposition and find Euclidean Strichartz estimates to hold; see Proposition
A.2
for the dispersive estimate. Correspondingly, the interval
[0,N^{-1}]
has to be split into intervals
I^{\prime}
of length
|I^{\prime}|=N^{-2\sigma}
to apply Euclidean Strichartz estimates,

\|u\|_{L_{t}^{2}(I^{\prime},L^{q}_{x})}\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(I^{\prime},L^{2})}+\|g\|_{L^{1}(I^{\prime},L^{2})}

We can estimate the source term on the short intervals by

\displaystyle\|g\|_{L^{1}(I^{\prime},L^{2})}\lesssim

\displaystyle|I^{\prime}|\|a^{ij}_{c}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(I^{\prime},L^{2})}+\|f\|_{L^{1}(I^{\prime},L^{2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle N^{-2\sigma}N^{2-\sigma(1+s)}\|a^{ij}\|_{C^{1}_{t}C_{x}^{1,s}}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}+\|f\|_{L^{1}(I^{\prime},L^{2})}

This leads to

\|u\|_{L_{t}^{2}(I^{\prime},L^{q}_{x})}\lesssim(1+N^{2-\sigma(3+s)})\|u\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}+\|f\|_{L^{1}(I^{\prime},L^{2})}

The two contributions balanced for
\sigma=\frac{2}{3+s}
, which gives

\|u\|_{L_{t}^{2}(I^{\prime},L^{q}_{x})}\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}}+\|f\|_{L^{1}(I^{\prime},L^{2})}

Since the number of intervals
I^{\prime}
is
N^{2\sigma-1}
,
summing over all
I^{\prime}\subset[0,N^{-1}]
yields
derivative loss
\kappa=\sigma-\frac{1}{2}=\frac{1-s}{2(3+s)}
as claimed.

∎

5.2.2.
Bilinear estimates

Next, we show a bilinear smoothing estimate on frequency-dependent times for Schrödinger equations with rough coefficients. Consider solutions to the system

\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}i\partial_{t}u_{1}+\partial_{i}g^{ij}_{1,\ll N}\partial_{j}u_{1}&=f_{1},\\
i\partial_{t}u_{2}+\partial_{i}g^{ij}_{2,\ll K}\partial_{j}u_{2}&=f_{2}\end{array}\right.

with

\text{supp}(\hat{u}_{1})\subseteq B(0,N)\backslash B(0,N/4),\quad\text{supp}(\hat{u}_{2})\subseteq B(0,K)

and
K\ll N
.

Theorem 5.3
