(Bilinear smoothing estimate)
.

Let
d\geq 1
, and
u_{i}
like above. We assume that
g^{ij}_{k}\in C^{0,1}_{t}C^{1,s}_{x}
is uniformly elliptic like in (
5.103
). Then the following estimate holds:

\|u_{1}u_{2}\|_{L^{\frac{d+3}{d+1}}_{t,x}([0,N^{-1}]\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim_{\varepsilon}N^{\kappa_{0}+\kappa_{1}+\varepsilon}\prod_{i=1}^{2}(\|u_{i}(0)\|_{L^{2}}+\|f_{i}\|_{L^{1}L^{2}})

with
\kappa_{0}=-\frac{2}{d+3}
and
\kappa_{1}=\frac{(d+1)(1-s)}{(3+s)(d+3)}
.

Proof.

An energy estimate reduces the claim to

(5.115)

\|u_{1}u_{2}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{d+3}{d+3}}([0,N^{-1}]\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim_{\varepsilon}N^{\kappa_{0}+\kappa_{1}+\varepsilon}\prod_{i=1}^{2}\big(\|u_{i}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{2}}+\|f_{i}\|_{L_{t}^{1}L_{x}^{2}}\big).

We use the same paradifferential truncation like before:

i\partial_{t}u_{k}+\partial_{i}g^{ij}_{k,\leq N,\leq N^{\sigma}}\partial_{i}u_{k}=-\partial_{i}g^{ij}_{k,c}\partial_{j}u_{k}+f_{k}

and note that we have wave packet decompositions at disposal on times
N^{-2\sigma}
, which was verified in the proof of Theorem
5.2
. Now we check the transversality assumptions. After rescaling to unit frequencies, we have
\text{supp}(\hat{u}_{1N})\subseteq B(0,2)\backslash B(0,1/2)
and
\text{supp}(\hat{u}_{2N})\subseteq B(0,K/N)
. Regarding the difference of the group velocities we see that

\begin{split}|\nabla_{\xi}p_{1}(z,\xi_{1})-\nabla_{\xi}p_{2}(z,\xi_{2})|&=|(g^{ij}_{1}\xi_{1j}-g^{ij}_{2}\xi_{2j})\mathbf{e}_{i}|\\
&\geq||g^{ij}_{1}\xi_{1j}|-|g^{ij}_{2}\xi_{2j}||\gtrsim 1-K/N\sim 1.\end{split}

Next, we turn to the second order transversality:
Here we use the ellipticity of
g^{ij}_{k}
, for
k=1,2
to estimate

\begin{split}&\quad(\partial_{\xi_{i}}p_{1}(z,\xi_{1})-\partial_{\xi_{i}}p_{2}(z,\xi_{2}))g^{ij}_{k}(\partial_{\xi_{j}}p_{1}(z,\xi_{1})-\partial_{\xi_{j}}p_{2}(z,\xi_{2}))\\
&\sim|\nabla_{\xi}p_{1}(z,\xi_{1})-\nabla_{\xi}p_{2}(z,\xi_{2})|\sim 1.\end{split}

Consequently, after the above reductions using finite speed of propagation, we can invoke Theorem
2.13
on intervals
I
of length
N^{-2\sigma}
and obtain on the time scale
N^{-2\sigma}
the Euclidean estimate:

\|u_{1}u_{2}\|_{L^{\frac{d+3}{d+1}}_{t,x}(I\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim_{\varepsilon}N^{-\frac{2}{d+3}+\varepsilon}\prod_{k=1}^{2}\big(\|u_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}_{I}L^{2}_{x}}+\|\partial_{i}g^{ij}_{k,c}\partial_{j}u_{k}\|_{L_{I}^{1}L^{2}_{x}}+\|f_{k}\|_{L_{I}^{1}L_{x}^{2}}\big).

We balance the estimate like above for
\sigma=\frac{2}{3+s}

\|u_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}_{I}L^{2}_{x}}+\|\partial_{i}g^{ij}_{k,c}\partial_{j}u_{k}\|_{L^{1}_{I}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim(1+N^{1-2\sigma}N^{1-\sigma(1+s)})\|u_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}_{I}L^{2}_{x}}.

The proof of (
5.115
) is concluded by carrying out the
\ell^{\frac{d+3}{d+1}}_{I}
-summation over intervals of length
|I|=N^{-2\sigma}
. This finishes the proof of Theorem
5.3
.
∎

Appendix A
Dispersive properties via wave packet decompositions

In the Appendix we obtain dispersive properties via wave packet decompositions. To prove the sharp decay for Schrödinger equations with uniformly non-degenerate metric, we use the non-degeneracy of the Hamiltonian flow in all directions.
We show the following:

Proposition A.1
.

Let
p(x,t,\xi)=g^{ij}(x,t)\xi_{i}\xi_{j}
with the metric satisfying the regularity assumption

(1.116)

|\partial_{z}^{\alpha}g^{ij}(x,t)|\leq\epsilon R^{-|\alpha|},\quad 1\leq|\alpha|\leq 2,

and uniform non-degeneracy

(1.117)

\exists c,C>0:c|\xi|\leq|g^{ij}\xi_{i}|\leq C|\xi|

with
\epsilon\ll c
and
\chi
a bump function adapted to
B_{d}(0,1)
. Then it holds for two bicharacteristics with
|\xi_{i}|\leq\frac{1}{2}
with
x_{0}^{1}=x_{0}^{2}=x_{0}
and
\xi_{0}^{i}=\xi_{i}
for
0\leq|t|\leq R
with
\Delta\xi=\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}
:

|x_{t}^{1}-x_{t}^{2}|\sim|t||\Delta\xi|,\quad|\xi_{t}^{1}-\xi_{t}^{2}|\sim|\Delta\xi|.

Proof.

In the first step we show upper bounds

|x_{t}^{1}-x_{t}^{2}|\lesssim|t||\Delta\xi|,\quad|\xi_{t}^{1}-\xi_{t}^{2}|\lesssim|\Delta\xi|.

This will be proved by bootstrap:
|\xi_{t}^{1}-\xi_{t}^{2}|\lesssim|\Delta\xi|
is clearly true by

\xi_{t}^{1}-\xi_{t}^{2}=\Delta\xi-\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\partial p}{\partial x}(x_{s}^{1},\xi_{s}^{1})-\frac{\partial p}{\partial x}(x_{s}^{2},\xi_{s}^{2})ds

for
t_{0}\sim|\Delta\xi|
.
Next, we expand

(1.118)

\begin{split}x_{t}^{1}-x_{t}^{2}&=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\partial p}{\partial\xi}(x_{s}^{1},\xi_{s}^{1})-\frac{\partial p}{\partial\xi}(x_{s}^{2},\xi_{s}^{2})ds\\
&=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\partial^{2}p}{\partial x\partial\xi}(x_{s}^{1}-x_{s}^{2})+\frac{\partial^{2}p}{(\partial\xi)^{2}}(\xi_{s}^{1}-\xi_{s}^{2})ds.\end{split}

Clearly, by the first identity
|x_{t}^{1}-x_{t}^{2}|\leq C|t|
and by the second,

|x_{t}^{1}-x_{t}^{2}|\leq\frac{C_{1}\epsilon}{R}|t|^{2}+C_{2}|t||\Delta\xi|.

Choosing
|t|
small enough we have for
0\leq|t|\leq t_{0}
the estimates
|x_{t}^{1}-x_{t}^{2}|\leq C|t||\Delta\xi|
,
|\xi_{t}^{1}-\xi_{t}^{2}|\leq C|\Delta\xi|
. This can be bootstrapped: Plugging theses estimates into the second identity we find

|x_{t}^{1}-x_{t}^{2}|\leq\frac{C\epsilon|t|^{2}}{2R}|\Delta\xi|+C|t||\Delta\xi|,

and for the frequencies

\begin{split}|\xi_{t}^{1}-\xi_{t}^{2}|&\leq|\Delta\xi|+\int_{0}^{t}\big|\frac{\partial^{2}p}{(\partial x)^{2}}(x_{s}^{1}-x_{s}^{2})ds\big|+\big|\frac{\partial^{2}p}{\partial x\partial\xi}(\xi_{s}^{1}-\xi_{s}^{2})\big|ds\\
&\leq|\Delta\xi|+\frac{C|t|^{2}\epsilon|\Delta\xi|}{R^{2}}+\frac{C\epsilon|t|}{R}|\Delta\xi|.\end{split}

This allows us to carry on the bootstrap up to
t_{0}=R
. For
\epsilon\ll 1
the bounds can be bootstrapped to
C=2
and moreover for
\epsilon\ll 1
the estimate shows that
|\xi_{t}^{1}-\xi_{t}^{2}|\sim|\Delta\xi|
.

Next we estimate
|x_{t}^{1}-x_{t}^{2}|\sim|t||\Delta\xi|
. In (
1.118
) we estimate the first term by

\big|\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\partial^{2}p}{\partial x\partial\xi}(x_{s}^{1}-x_{s}^{2})ds\big|\leq\frac{|t|^{2}\epsilon|\Delta\xi|}{R}

and the second term as

\big|\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\partial^{2}p}{(\partial\xi)^{2}}(\xi_{s}^{1}-\xi_{s}^{2})\big|=|t|\big|\frac{\partial^{2}p}{(\partial\xi)^{2}}(\xi_{t_{*}}^{1}-\xi_{t_{*}}^{2})\big|\geq c|t||\Delta\xi|.

Consequently, for
\epsilon
small compared to
c
from the uniform non-degeneracy assumption, we find
|\Delta x_{t}|\sim|t||\Delta\xi|
.
∎

We are ready for the proof of dispersive estimates for non-degenerate Schrödinger equations:

Proposition A.2
.

Let
p(x,t,\xi)=g^{ij}(x,t)\xi_{i}\xi_{j}\chi(\xi)
with metric satisfying the estimates

|\partial_{z}^{\alpha}g^{ij}(x,t)|\leq\begin{cases}\epsilon R^{-|\alpha|},&\quad 1\leq|\alpha|\leq 2,\\
C_{\alpha}R^{-2-\frac{(|\alpha|-2)_{+}}{2}},&\quad|\alpha|\geq 3,\end{cases}

\chi
a bump function localizing to the unit ball,
\mathcal{Y}=B_{d}(0,R)\times B_{d}(0,1)
and
u
be a solution to

\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}i\partial_{t}u+p^{w}(x,t;D)u&=0,\quad(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d},\\
u(0)&=\chi_{\mathcal{Y}}f.\end{array}\right.

Above
\chi_{\mathcal{Y}}(x,D)
is a pseudo-differential operator localizing to
\mathcal{Y}
on the scales
R\times 1
and
f
localized to unit frequencies. Then it holds the dispersive estimate:

\|u(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim(1+|t|)^{-\frac{d}{2}}\|f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Remark A.3
.

We recover the above regularity of a flow as follows: Consider
g^{ij}\in C^{0,1}_{t}C^{1,1}_{x}
with solutions localized at frequencies
N
, the coefficients being paradifferentially truncated at
\leq N
in time and
\leq N^{\frac{1}{2}}
in spatial frequencies on time-scales. On times
T=T(N)=N^{-1}
we obtain (
1.116
) after parabolic rescaling
t\to N^{2}t
and
x\to Nx
with
R=N
.

Proof.

We plug in the wave packet decomposition at scale
|t|\sim r\gg 1
:

u(t)=\sum_{T\in\Lambda_{r}\cap\overline{\mathcal{Y}}}\chi_{T}+\text{RapDec}(r)\|f\|_{L^{2}}.

To ease notation, we let again
R=r
. Since we assumed
f
to be micro-localized to unit frequencies and essentially the
R
-ball we further have
\|f\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{1}}
. It remains to understand
\big\|\sum_{T\in\Lambda_{R}\cap\overline{\mathcal{Y}}}\chi_{T}\big\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{R})}
. To this end, we use the localization properties to
R^{\frac{1}{2}}
-squares and suffices to consider an
R^{\frac{1}{2}}
-square:

\begin{split}&\quad\big\|\sum_{T\in\Lambda_{R}\cap\overline{\mathcal{Y}}}\chi_{T}\big\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{R^{\frac{1}{2}}})}\\
&\lesssim\sum_{v\in R^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{d}\cap Q_{R}}\sum_{\theta\in R^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{d}\cap B_{d}(0,1)}R^{-\frac{d}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{-\frac{\rho(y-v)^{2}}{2}}|\chi_{\mathcal{Y}}f|dy\\
&\quad\quad\times(1+R^{-\frac{1}{2}}(|x_{q}-x_{\theta,v}(R)|)^{-(d+1)}\\
&\lesssim R^{-\frac{d}{2}}\sum_{v\in R^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\|\chi_{v}\chi_{\mathcal{Y}}f\|_{L^{1}}\sum_{\theta\in R^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{d}}(1+R^{-\frac{1}{2}}|x_{q}-x_{\theta,v}(R)|)^{-(d+1)}\\
&\lesssim R^{-\frac{d}{2}}\|\chi_{\mathcal{Y}}f\|_{L^{1}}.\end{split}

In the final line we used that for fixed
v\in R^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{d}
we have
|x_{(\theta,v)}(R)-x_{(\theta^{\prime},v)}(R)|\sim R|\theta-\theta^{\prime}|
by Proposition
A.1
.
∎

To obtain an analog result for wave equations we show the separation property of the Hamiltonian flow in the angular direction.

Proposition A.4
.

Let
p(x,t,\xi)=(g^{ij}(x,t,\xi)\xi_{i}\xi_{j})^{\frac{1}{2}}\chi_{1}(\xi)
with uniformly elliptic metric and the coefficients satisfying

|\partial_{z}^{\alpha}g^{ij}(x,t)|\leq\epsilon R^{-|\alpha|},\quad 1\leq|\alpha|\leq 2.

Then it holds for bicharacteristics with
|\xi_{i}|\sim 1
and
x_{0}=x_{0}^{1}=x_{0}^{2}
and
\xi_{0}^{i}=\xi_{i}
for
0\leq|t|\leq R
:

|x_{t}^{1}-x_{t}^{2}|\sim|t||\angle(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})|,\quad|\angle(\xi_{t}^{1},\xi_{t}^{2})|\sim|\angle(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})|.

With this property of the flow at hand, it is not difficult to show the dispersive property.

Proposition A.5
.

Let
p
be like in Proposition
A.4
with coefficients

|\partial_{z}^{\alpha}g^{ij}(x,t)|\leq\begin{cases}\epsilon R^{-|\alpha|},&\quad 1\leq|\alpha|\leq 2,\\
C_{\alpha}R^{-2-\frac{(|\alpha|-2)_{+}}{2}},&\quad|\alpha|\geq 3,\end{cases}

and
\mathcal{Y}=B_{d}(0,R)\times A_{d}
, and
u
be a solution to

\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}i\partial_{t}u+p^{w}(x,t;D)u&=0,\quad(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d},\\
u(0)&=\chi_{\mathcal{Y}}f.\end{array}\right.

Above
\chi_{\mathcal{Y}}(x,D)
is a pseudo-differential operator, which localizes to
\mathcal{Y}
on scales
R\times 1
. Then it holds the dispersive property:

\|u(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim(1+|t|)^{-\frac{d-1}{2}}\|f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Remark A.6
.

This corresponds to metric coefficients
g^{ij}\in C^{1,1}_{t,x}
on a unit time scale and frequency-localized solutions at frequency
N
after paradifferential decomposition at frequencies
\leq N^{\frac{1}{2}}
and rescaling
(x,t)\to N(x,t)
.

Proof.

Like in Proposition
A.2
we plug in the wave packet decomposition to find

u(t)=\sum_{T\in\Lambda_{R}\cap\overline{\mathcal{Y}}}\chi_{T}+\text{RapDec}(R)\|f\|_{L^{2}}.

For the main term from the wave packet decomposition we have

\begin{split}&\quad\big\|\sum_{T\in\Lambda_{R}\cap\overline{\mathcal{Y}}}\chi_{T}\big\|_{L^{\infty}(Q_{R^{\frac{1}{2}}})}\\
&\lesssim R^{-\frac{d}{2}}\sum_{v\in R^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\|f\chi_{v}\|_{L^{1}}\sum_{\theta\in R^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{d}\cap\bar{A}_{d}}(1+R^{-\frac{1}{2}}|x_{q}-x_{(\theta,v)}(R)|)^{-(d+1)}\\
&\lesssim R^{-\frac{d-1}{2}}\|f\|_{L^{1}}.\end{split}

∎
